Accepted inputs and the unchanged problem\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite dimension,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimizer x_star,\nand x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is fixed.\nThus W=2R. The original question is whether\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1.\n\nThe benchmark is the original ALL-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, the independent-vector certificate interpretation, and the\nexact incoming-terminal-free completion. It removes NO prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-word benchmark comparison\nB_n/D<47/50 and its CONDITIONAL implication from W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D).\nThat full-class upper hypothesis remains unproved. Its elementary mass\nformula is also available. No other mathematical fact is a premise here.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536. All hierarchy symbols in this report are local.\nThe physical step at i is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2 is the exponent\nof 2 dividing a positive integer. Put Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and\nt_i=1+Ppos_i for 0<=i<n. The column t is an auxiliary clock, not time\nor a gradient. A quadratic y^T K y for vector entries means\nsum_(i,j) K_ij <y_i,y_j>.\n\nThe following elementary bounds will suffice:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,\n    4/3<b<3/2, n>=15, S>n.\n\nIndeed sqrt(2)>7/5, and sqrt(2)<17/12 yields\nell<29/12; multiplying by 1+1/6144 still gives c<5/2.\nThen a>(7/8)/(5/2)=7/20>1/3; and a<1/ell<1/2.\nEvery step exceeds one. The displayed n bound follows from m>=1536.\n\n## 3. Full original coordinates; no projection or quotient\n\nNormalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain\nARBITRARY independent vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n in a real Euclidean space.\nEvery position always means the polynomial\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nFor distinct original labels define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nActual legal jets obey Q_ij<=0. A certificate at level q82 requires\nlambda_ij>=0, finite divergence out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and PSD of\n\n    M(q82)=q82||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n\non ALL these independent vectors after the value cancellation.\n\nFor each queried i<n define y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nSet y_star=delta_star=0. Define the lower triangular matrix T82 by\n\n    T82_ii=1, T82_it=h_t for t<i, T82_it=0 for t>i.\n\nWrite g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1))^T and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1))^T; in matrix\nproducts h also denotes its column. As vector-column identities,\n\n    y=e*x_0-T82g,  g=T82^(-1)(e*x_0-y),  T82e=t.             (3.1)\n\nT82 has diagonal one, so (x_0,y_0,...,y_(n-1),g_n) and the original\nvariables are in invertible correspondence. In particular y is an\narbitrary n-tuple once x_0 is fixed. No equal-gradient relation is imposed.\n\nWritten finite products define\n\n    pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t),\n    k_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t), 0<=i<n.\n\nIn particular the inverse in (3.1) has the literal vector formula\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j*product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j. (3.2)\n\nFor the second identity, the coefficient sum on a suffix equals one\nminus the suffix product: expand each h_i=1-(1-h_i) and telescope.\nThus k_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i, proving the last identity.\n\nThere are n physical factors 1-h_i=-a*c^v2(i+1). Also\nsum_(i<n)v2(i+1)=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m.\nSince n is odd, this proves the EXACT full-word product\n\n    pi_n=-s82,  s82=beta^n/c^m>0.                        (3.3)\n\nOnly an identity for the literal physical word is asserted; s82 is not\nbeing substituted for a worst-case contraction factor.\n\nChoose a parameter p82 with s82<p82<1, and a probability distribution\nalpha_i>=0 on queried indices with sum_i alpha_i=1-p82. The terminal\nweight at the optimum will be alpha_star=p82. Define\n\n    v=k-alpha,\n    w_i=p82+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*w_i-alpha_i,\n    U82=sum_i Rvec_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2*p82/(p82+s82), eta=(p82-s82)/(p82+s82).                  (3.4)\n\nThen\n\n    e^T v=p82+s82, T82^T v=Rvec, gamma=1+eta,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*(p82+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.\n\nThese identities follow from (3.2) and sum alpha=1-p82. Signed coefficient matching and its exact full identity\n\nThis section gives a literal algebraic parameterization of the two laws,\nincluding the entries that FAIL nonnegativity. It is not a claim that a\npositive square factor necessarily has nonnegative row coefficients.\n\nLet K be a specified symmetric matrix with Ke=0. In the two laws K is\ngiven by explicit positive squares, never by an unknown PSD condition.\nLet A be a specified skew matrix, A^T=-A, and put d=Ae. Thus e^T d=0.\nDefine the fully specified matrices\n\n    Pmat=K+gamma*v*v^T,\n    Nmat=T82^T(Pmat+A).\n\nTo make clear that this is a finite coefficient formula, each entry is\n\n    Nmat_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*Rvec_i*v_j.   (4.1)\n\nThe FULL original signed row array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i, i<n,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij, i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Nmat e)_i=2*p82*Rvec_i+(T82^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(Nmat^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(K t)_i+gamma*U82*v_i-(A t)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                      (4.2)\n\nThis lists every entry; no additional rows, diagonal multipliers, or\ncompletion freedom are presumed. Finite sums are definitions, not\nnumerical or recurrence executions.\n\nAll value-flow cancellations hold even before sign checking. At a prefix\nnode the total outgoing coefficient is Nmat_ii, and total incoming\nPREFIX coefficient is Nmat_ii-2alpha_i. Thus prefix divergence is\n2alpha_i. The incoming terminal edge has coefficient 2alpha_i, giving\nzero divergence at that original queried node. Terminal divergence is\n2*p82+2sum alpha_i=2. The optimum divergence is -2. All function values\ncancel in M(q82), without imposing any relation among them.\n\nDirect expansion yields Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i> for prefix\nindices, including star. From (3.1),\n\n    -g^T Nmat y\n       =-<x_0,e^T(Pmat+A)y>+y^T Pmat y,\n    e^T Pmat=2*p82*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, or direct expansion of the displayed outgoing\nterminal rows, now gives the ENTIRE original identity\n\n    M(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2+R(q82),\n    R(q82)=y^T K y+eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n                         +(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.  (4.3)\n\nFor direct checking, before completing the common square the prefix form is\n\n    (q82-s82^2)||x_0||^2+2(s82-p82)<x_0,v^T y>\n                              +eta||v^T y||^2+y^TKy+<x_0,d^Ty>.\n\nThe equality eta(p82+s82)=p82-s82 completes it to (4.3). This is an identity\non arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.\n\nThe common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried\ngradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and\nv^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is\n\n    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>\n                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)\n\nIn particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in\nG at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and\ntheir true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.\n\n## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries\n\nUse uniform queried terminal weights\n\n    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)\n\nFor each actual dyadic cell\n\n    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},\n    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),\n\nlet Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,\neach of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.\nDefine two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:\n\n    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),\n    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)\n                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)\n\nThe first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second\nretains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and\nnorm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum\nzero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all\nthese columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two\nchildren contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute\ntwo, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.\nThe singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently\nthere are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form\nan orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.\nExplicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is\n{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is\n{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.\n\nThe literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,\n\n    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),\n    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),\n    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T\n                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)\n\nEvery square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The\nstronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega\nat every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.\nThat extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.\n\nEquations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law\nmu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming\noptimum coefficients are literally\n\n    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)\n                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)\n\nThey are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.\nAn original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless\nof whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.\n\nFor every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is\n\n    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)\n\nThis supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and\neta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;\nat q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child\nPSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement\nwill be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.\n\n### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained\n\nLet omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.\nOnly those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0\nand root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,\n\n    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),\n    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)\n\nKe=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.\nHere Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore\n\n    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)\n                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,\n    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)\n\nThe last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix\nequal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product\ncalculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives\n\n    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,\n    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)\n\nConsequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives\n\n    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0.       (I.9)\n\nThis identifies a negative ORIGINAL multiplier for every parameter choice:\nif E_z>0, lambda_(0,z)=-E_z<0; if E_z<=0, (I.9) forces\nlambda_(0,r)=-E_r<0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.\nThe conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,\nevery kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail\nweights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter\nthe sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).\n\nThe excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not\nincoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.\n\n### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail\n\nThere is a useful stronger scope statement. Keep the exact factor (I.5)\nand exact terminal distribution (I.1), but allow any other original prefix\nrow coefficients matching that polynomial. If their row matrix is Nnew,\nput Bnew=T82^(-T)Nnew-Pmat. Comparing y-y and x_0-y coefficients in (4.3)\nforces Bnew to be skew and e^T Bnew=0, equivalently Bnew e=0. Conversely\nevery such balanced-skew term leaves the same polynomial unchanged.\nThus this argument describes all coefficient re-selections of this fixed\nfactor, rather than assuming the symmetric matching is unique.\n\nFor any balanced-skew Bnew, the first diagonal change is\n\n    (T82^T Bnew)_00=(1-b)Bnew_00+b(e^T Bnew)_0=0,\n\nand the first row-sum change is zero. Hence the sum of its first-row\noff-diagonal N entries is invariant and equals\n\n    mu_0-Nmat_00\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+s82-v_0)\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+u-s82/a).                (I.10)\n\nHere k_0=b*s82/a, since pi_n=-s82=(1-b)*product_(1<=t<n)(1-h_t).\nThe last factor is strictly positive throughout the parameter domain.\nIndeed p82+(1-p82)/n is increasing in p82; at p82=s82,\n\n    n*(p82+u-s82/a)=1-s82*(n*(1/a-1)+1)>0.\n\nFor the strict last bound, a>1/3 gives n*(1/a-1)+1<2n+1<2^(m+1),\nand s82<c^(-m) gives its product with s82 less than 2*(5/6)^m<1.\nThe inequality (6/5)^4=1296/625>2 proves this for every m>=4.\nAlso K_00>0, because the complete basis has a nonzero contrast at 0\nand every detail weight is positive. Thus (I.10)>0.\n\nIf ALL original lambda_(0,j) were nonnegative, then all off-diagonal\nNnew_0j would be nonpositive and their sum could not be positive.\nThis contradiction excludes EVERY nonnegative coefficient matching of\nthis particular full square factor and its fixed terminal distribution.\nIt does not exclude a changed square factor or a changed terminal pool.\n\n## 6. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction\n\nChoose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices\n\n    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,\n    alpha_star=p82.                                      (II.1)\n\nIts sum is 1-p82 because sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(i+1)=n(n+1)/2.\nIts chronological cumulative is\n\n    w_i=p82+(1-p82)(i+1)(i+2)/[n(n+1)].\n\nThis is not a distribution on a handful of selected terminals. Every\ngenuine prefix sample participates; every physical leaf and checkpoint\nis still independent. In particular alpha_i<2/n.\n\nChoose kappa>0 and tau>=0, and set\n\n    K=kappa*(I-e e^T/n),\n    d=tau*(e_z-e_0),\n    A=(d e^T-e d^T)/n.                               (II.2)\n\nThen A^T=-A and Ae=d. This is a literal dense chronology correction,\nnot an unidentified circulation or an uncharged antisymmetric term.\nThe imbalance at the original first and last queried samples changes\nthe common-gradient derivative and the optimum coefficients.\n\nFor a completely scalar specification, put\n\n    Cclock_i=1+h_i*(n-1-i),\n    dtilde_0=a*tau,\n    dtilde_i=h_i*tau for 0<i<z,\n    dtilde_z=tau,\n    tbar=(sum_i t_i)/n.\n\nAll original finite coefficients come from the literal formula\n\n    Nmat_ij=kappa*(1_(i=j)+h_i*1_(j>i)-Cclock_i/n)\n            +gamma Rvec_i v_j+(dtilde_i-Cclock_i*d_j)/n,\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij for i!=j<n.                 (II.3)\n\nThe optimum coefficients and terminal coefficients are\n\n    mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i+dtilde_i>0,\n    nu_i=kappa*(t_i-tbar)+gamma U82 v_i-d_i*tbar\n                                      +tau*Ppos_z/n-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, lambda_(i,i)=0.                   (II.4)\n\nThey follow from (4.2), using d^T t=tau*Ppos_z. This specifies ALL\nordered entries, including both optimum orientations. The nu entries\nremain signed matching expressions; their signs are not assumed.\nThe value-flow calculation of Section 4 applies without modification.\n\nWrite ybar=(sum_i y_i)/n. Completing ALL detail squares in (4.3) gives\nthe single global identity\n\n    M_II(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +kappa*sum_(i<n)||y_i-ybar+(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n      +(q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa))*||x_0||^2.             (II.5)\n\nSince sum d_i=0 and sum d_i^2=2tau^2, expanding the detail squares\nrecovers exactly kappa sum||y_i-ybar||^2+<x_0,d^T y> and the displayed\ncharge. There is no sum of independently minimized local Schur budgets.\nThe common-gradient restriction, in particular, is\n\n    R_II,common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-tau*Ppos_z*<x_0,G>\n            +[kappa sum_i(t_i-tbar)^2+eta U82^2]||G||^2.  (II.6)\n\nIt has the nonzero derivative -tau*Ppos_z*x_0 at G=0. Discarding that\nderivative would discard precisely the payment this construction makes.\n\nFull PSD of (II.5) is EQUIVALENT to\n\n    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa).                            (II.7)\n\nSufficiency follows from the explicit squares. For necessity take any\nnonzero scalar x_0, set y_i-ybar=-(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0, and select the\ncommon value ybar so that v^T y=(p82+s82)x_0. This is possible because\nsum v_i=p82+s82>0. Finally set g_n=-s82*x_0+v^T y. These are arbitrary\nformal coordinates under the invertible map (3.1). They annihilate every\nother square. A negative last coefficient would therefore give an\nexplicit negative original-space direction. No interpolability is claimed.\nMore explicitly, take x_0=1,\ny_i=1+(v^T d)/(2kappa(p82+s82))-d_i/(2kappa), g_n=p82,\nand use (3.1a) for each queried g_i. The value of the whole polynomial is\nthen q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa).\n\n### 6.1 Two literal original signs exceed the full charge budget\n\nLet j=z-2 be the preceding queried leaf. The final three physical steps\nare exactly (b,1+beta,b), so\n\n    k_z=b, k_j=b*a*beta.\n\nBy (II.1), alpha_i<2/n. Since n>=15 and b>4/3,\n\n    v_z>b-2/n>1,\n    v_j>b*a*beta-2/n\n         >7/18-2/15=23/90>1/4.                      (II.8)\n\nAlso gamma>1, Rvec_0>0. The final-row entry (II.3) becomes\n\n    Nmat_zj=(-kappa+tau)/n+gamma v_z v_j.             (II.9)\n\nIf lambda_(z,j)=-Nmat_zj>=0, then\n\n    kappa>=tau+n*gamma v_z v_j>tau+n/4>=n/4.          (II.10)\n\nThe first-to-last queried entry is exactly\n\n    Nmat_0z=a*kappa/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_z\n                                      -(b-2a/n)*tau. (II.11)\n\nThe factor b-2a/n is positive. If lambda_(0,z)>=0, (II.11) implies\n\n    b*tau>a*kappa/n,\n    tau>(a/b)*kappa/n>kappa/(4n).                    (II.12)\n\nThe last inequality follows from a=b-1 and b>4/3. The dropped terms\ngamma Rvec_0 v_z and 2a*tau/n were nonnegative, with the first strictly\npositive, so the strict comparison in (II.12) is justified.\n\nCombining (II.7), (II.10), and (II.12) gives\n\n    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa)\n      >kappa/(32n^2)>1/(128n).                       (II.13)\n\nThis is a parameter-independent bound for this literal law. At any\nq82<=1/(128n), either its exact PSD condition fails, or one of the two\nexplicit original multipliers lambda_(z,z-2),lambda_(0,z) is negative.\nIf the PSD condition is imposed, an ordered sign audit is explicit:\ntest (II.9); if it is positive, lambda_(z,z-2)<0. If it is nonpositive,\n(II.10) holds and (II.7) forces tau<=sqrt(2kappa q82); at the forbidden\nlevel the inequalities used in (II.12) cannot hold, so (II.11)>0 and\nlambda_(0,z)<0. No claim is made that all the remaining signs pass.\n\nThis second failure is for the displayed A in (II.2) and complete literal\narray. A further balanced-skew term preserving this same factor may\nchange the two selected entries; unlike Section 5.2, no exclusion of\nthat enlarged second matching family is asserted.\n\n## 7. All-depth comparison with the target; nonempty factor domains\n\nThe hierarchy mass can be checked directly. Put\n\n    vword=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)], Aword=1+S.\n\nDefine A_j=1+sum H_j. The exact solution of\nA_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j, A_0=1 is\n\n    A_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j, 0<=j<=m.             (7.1)\n\nSubstitution proves the formula at every depth; it is not an executed\nrecurrence. Since c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1, one has 0<vword<1.\nFurthermore\n\n    c(c-2)=1+2(ell+1)eps+ell^2 eps^2<1+7eps+6eps^2,\n    (1-8eps)(1+7eps+6eps^2)\n                       =1-eps-50eps^2-48eps^3<1-eps.\n\nThus vword>1-8eps=1-2/m>1/2. Equation (7.1) implies\n\n    Aword<c^m, D=2Aword-1>c^m-1,\n    D/n>(c/2)^m>(6/5)^m.                            (7.2)\n\nFor the middle strict ratio, (c^m-1)/(2^m-1)>c^m/2^m follows by\ncross multiplying and using c^m>2^m. The binomial expansion gives\n\n    (6/5)^8>1+8/5+28/25+56/125=521/125>4.\n\nRaising to the fourth power yields (6/5)^32>256. Therefore EVERY\nm>=1536 satisfies D>256n>136n, and hence\n\n    qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).                          (7.3)\n\nThe factor domains are not vacuous. Because s82=beta^n/c^m and\nAword<c^m, s82*Aword<1. Thus pbase82=1/Aword belongs to (s82,1). Moreover\n\n    17*Aword^2-16*(2Aword-1)\n                               =16*(Aword-1)^2+Aword^2>0\n\nproves pbase82^2<qtar82. Law I therefore has strictly positive full factors at\nthe target, for example p82=pbase82 and kappa=theta=1. Their matched row signs\nfail by Section 5. For Law II, p82=pbase82 and tau=0 also give a full PSD\nfactor at qtar82 for every kappa>0, but the literal row signs fail. Choosing\ntau>0 attempts to repair signs; (II.13) proves its full common/initial\ncost exceeds the target before the two indicated signs can both pass.\n\nAll comparisons above are symbolic inequalities valid throughout the\nenclosing-m domain. No m-dependent child reset, dropped terminal step,\nnumerical asymptotic estimate, or replacement benchmark is used.\n\n## 8. Every original queried gradient,\nthe actual unqueried terminal gradient, and the initial vector remain\nindependent. The two explicit laws use the same weighted full square and\ndistributed imbalance, but genuinely different original coefficient arrays.\n\nLaw A uses the canonical weighted skew for that imbalance. Throughout its\nentire domain, its incoming optimum multiplier at original query1 is\nnegative. Independently, making its first-to-last queried multiplier\nnonnegative forces its exact global square charge above the assigned\nqtar=17/(16D). This quantifies why paying through an exponentially tiny\ninitial tail weight fails in this particular matching.\n\nLaw B adds an EXPLICIT BALANCED skew transport, with no sign-feasibility\noracle. It equalizes the entire first row, and can make all its finite\nmultipliers nonnegative while maintaining the full PSD factor at qtar.\nNevertheless, for EVERY member of Law B, either the original multiplier\nfrom the last queried leaf to the preceding queried leaf is negative, or\nthe original incoming optimum multiplier at query1 is negative. This is\nan exhaustive obstruction for this displayed balanced correction, including\narbitrary positive metric scale and arbitrary nonnegative imbalance scale.\n\nNeither exclusion is an impossibility theorem for all balanced corrections,\nall weighted variances, all terminal probabilities, or the full dual cone.\nNo primal interpolability or lower bound on W is inferred from a formal\nnegative direction or a negative signed multiplier. No full upper bound on\nW, benchmark improvement, or resolution of the original problem is obtained.\n\n## 2. Original problem and accepted inputs\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, W_n(h) denotes\nthe supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth function with an\nattained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the original recursion\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The original benchmark retains EVERY integer split:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation, and exact\nincoming-terminal-free reduction. It deletes NO queried-prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-enclosing-m word below,\nits mass identity and the scalar comparison B_n/D<47/50. Its implication\nfrom W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) to a strict benchmark improvement is CONDITIONAL;\nthat full-class upper hypothesis is still missing. These are the only\naccepted mathematical inputs used here. Historical draft6e66efffac0795f1\nis recall only; its coordinate and row identities are rederived below.\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536. All children use this SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, z=n-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, Aword=1+S, D=1+2S=2Aword-1,\n    p=1/Aword, qtar=17/(16D), b=1+a.\n\nThe symbols p and q in this report mean p83 and q83; neither is a benchmark\nexponent or a primal derivative. The letter B without a subscript below is\na skew matrix, not the benchmark B_n. All other hierarchy notation is local.\nThe actual physical step is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2(j) is the exponent\nof2 dividing the positive integer j. The final three steps are\n(b,1+beta,b). The first and last queried steps equal b; index n is the\nACTUAL terminal, distinct from z.\n\nThe elementary bounds needed below are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.       (2.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<17/12; hence ell>12/5 and\nell<29/12, and (29/12)(1+1/6144)<5/2. Also beta>7/8,\na>(7/8)/(5/2)>1/3, and a<1/ell<1/2.\nThe accepted mass formula is\n\n    Aword=(1-vword)2^m+vword*c^m,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).\n\nIndeed c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1 give c(c-2)>1. Consequently\n\n    2^m<Aword<c^m, 0<p<2^(-m)<1/128.                  (2.2)\n\nEvery h_i>1, consistently with these strict mass inequalities.\n\n## 3. Exact physical suffix products and the selected terminal law\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum of a nonnegative integer L, including\nsbit(0)=0. For 0<=L<=n define the product of the FINAL L actual factors:\n\n    r_L=product_(t=n-L)^(n-1)(h_t-1), r_0=1.\n\nThe index j counted backward from the final physical step has\nh_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(2^m-j)=a*c^v2(j), for 1<=j<2^m. The valuation equality\nfollows by factoring the exact power of2 from j: the remaining difference\nfrom2^m is odd. For any L, sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t).\nWriting L in binary and summing each digit's contributions gives\nsum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t)=L-sbit(L). Therefore the EXACT suffix identity is\n\n    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), 0<=L<=n.                 (3.1)\n\nNo product or recurrence has been numerically evaluated. In particular\ns:=r_n=beta^n/c^m, and the signed full-word product is -s since n is odd.\nDefine the actual terminal coefficient and its absolute scale by\n\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_(i<n)u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIf L=n-1-i, adding the next backward factor shows\n\n    u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^L u_i=(-1)^i u_i.       (3.2)\n\nThus all u_i are strictly positive. The last three are\n\n    u_z=b, u_(z-1)=a(1+beta), u_(z-2)=b*a*beta,\n    u_0=(b/a)s.                                      (3.3)\n\nExpanding a product over the m binary digits gives the finite identity\n\n    Z=sum_(L=0)^n r_L=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Z-1-s <2(3/2)^m.                              (3.4)\n\nThe last inequality uses beta<1 and c>2. The three distinct last terms\nin (3.3), with (2.1), give\n\n    U>4/3+5/8+7/18=169/72>7/3.                       (3.5)\n\nBoth laws use these SAME explicit terminal probabilities:\n\n    alpha_star=p, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i=delta*u_i,\n    delta=(1-p)/U, rplus=1-delta, rminus=1+delta,\n    v=k-alpha.                                       (3.6)\n\nThey sum to1 and are strictly positive on every original queried sample\nand the optimum. They are probabilities on real original rows, not on a\nselected child interface. Since U>7/3, one has 0<delta<3/7, rplus>1/2,\nand 1<rminus<3/2. Precisely\n\n    v_i=rplus*u_i for i even,\n    v_i=-rminus*u_i for i odd.                        (3.7)\n\nSeveral uniform small-initial-weight bounds will be used. The binomial\nexpansion implies n=2^m-1>m(m-1)/2>128m for m>=1536. Also\n(1-eps)^(-n)>(1+eps)^n>=1+n*eps, so beta^n<1/33. From Aword<c^m,\ns/p=beta^n*Aword/c^m<beta^n. Because b/a=1+1/a<4,\n\n    s<p/33, u_0<4p/33<p/8,\n    alpha_0<p/8, rho_0<3p/56<1/10.                  (3.8)\n\nThese are symbolic inequalities uniform in all enclosing m in the task.\n\n## 4. Full original coordinates and the complete signed matching\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep arbitrary independent\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with every original position defined by\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nThe residual orientation is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal actual jets obey Q_ij<=0. For i<n let y_i=x_i-g_i and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, with the corresponding star quantities zero.\nLet e be the all-ones column of length n, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nT=T82 the lower triangular matrix T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i.\nFor vector columns g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1)) and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1)),\n\n    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y).                    (4.1)\n\nThis is invertible on the ENTIRE formal space. Explicitly, if\npi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), then\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (4.2)\n\nSubstitution into x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i proves\n\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.           (4.3)\n\nFor the latter identity, h_j=1-(1-h_j) telescopes on each suffix:\nk_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. In particular e^T v=p+s.\nThe cumulative terminal probabilities, distinct from detail weights below,\nare Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j. Define\n\n    R_i=h_i*Cprob_i-alpha_i,\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t=Te,\n    Uclock=sum_i R_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s).\n\nThen T^T v=R and\nR_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                  (4.7)\n\nSigns are not presumed. This lists every original coefficient, including\nboth optimum orientations and the actual outgoing terminal distribution.\nFor each prefix index i, the outgoing sum is N_ii, while the incoming\nPREFIX sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. The incoming terminal coefficient2alpha_i\ntherefore gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal divergence is2;\noptimum divergence is-2. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.  (5.2)\n\nThe charge J is paid ONCE globally. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15.                (6.7)\n\nThe strict lower bounds use a*b>4/9, gamma>1, R_0>bp, U>7/3 and\nrho_0<1/10. On the other hand Aword>128 gives\n\n    qtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]<8/15,             (6.8)\n\nbecause this inequality is equivalent to Aword>128. Thus no member of\nLaw A has both full PSD and lambda_(0,z)>=0 at q<=qtar. This argument\nuses the exact SINGLE global charge and no local Schur-budget summation.\n\n### 6.2 A stronger literal incoming-optimum failure for every q\n\nThe weighted clock is uniformly large. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. This excludes\nLaw A as an original-row certificate even at larger q. It does not exclude\na balanced re-selection, which can change nu_1 and (6.5). Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H.      (7.4)\n\nIn particular ALL original finite first-row multipliers are nonnegative\niff S0<=0, equivalently\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).           (7.5)\n\nThis realizes the invariant (5.4), rather than assuming it can be repaired.\nIt changes the demanding Law A quantity U-1+p in (6.6) to the much smaller\nH/(1-rho_0). Thus the Law A charge obstruction does NOT transfer to Law B.\n\nThe simultaneous first-row and full-PSD conditions at qtar are nonvacuous.\nWithin this same two-parameter law choose the explicit member\n\n    kappa_0=gamma*R_0*H/[a*u_0*(1-rho_0)],\n    tau_0=2*gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).                    (7.6)\n\nThese are positive. Equation (7.4) gives N^B_0j=0 for every j>0, and\nJ_A=a*gamma*R_0*H. From (2.1), gamma<2 and (4.4),\n\n    J_A< (25p/16)*(9p/8)=225p^2/128<2p^2.\n\nConsequently p^2+J_A<3p^2<p/2<qtar, because p<1/128 and\nqtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]>17/32>1/2. This is a strictly positive\nfull factor at the target with all finite first-row multipliers zero,\npositive terminal probabilities, and positive outgoing optimum multipliers.\nIt remains a FAILED certificate because the complete array still has a\nnegative original coefficient, as the next subsection proves.\n\n### 7.2 Last-to-previous-leaf versus incoming optimum1: exhaustive sign conflict\n\nLet j=z-2, the genuine preceding queried leaf. No new fact_id exists.\n\nThis report derives the full remainder when all middle gradients are\nreleased at j=0,k=n-2. It gives explicit nonnegative weights on actual\nrows for two natural lifts, and proves that neither lift is positive\nsemidefinite on the full formal space, even with the allowed objective\nslack. The second mechanism tests an explicit four-sector discriminator:\nits negative formal direction is not interpolable, and the most direct\nsingle-boundary-cycle repair cannot be PSD either. These are scoped\ncertificate obstructions, not a counterexample to a function-class bound.\n\nThe theorem-level interface remains open: a successful lift needs a\ndifferent original-row construction with a full-space sign/Schur proof.\nNo uniform upper bound on W, no primal escape, and no resolution of the\noriginal every-n/every-nonnegative-word problem is claimed.\n\nAll derivations below are joint: no new unverified lemma is imported as\nan accepted premise. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies only original\ninterpolation necessity/sufficiency and normalization. Accepted\n931b9653ce0f7201 supplies only its hierarchy/benchmark scope, in Section12.\n\n## 1. Same physical word, actual boundary indices, and normalization\n\nFix any integer m>=1536, and throughout set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, Z=D_m=1+2S.\n\nAll children keep this SAME enclosing m and all its parameters. Index\nthe actual steps from0 to n-1. Put\n\n    K=n-2, R=n-1,\n    A=h_0=h_R=1+a, d=a, e=h_K-1=a*c=beta_h,\n    I={1,...,K}, M=sum_(i=1)^(K-1) h_i,\n    B=sum_(i in I)h_i=M+1+e=S-2A,\n    v=M+1=B-e, U=1+d+v,\n    w=A+1/2-A^2/2=1-a^2/2,\n    D=U+e+w=S+1/2-A^2/2.\n\nThus 2D=Z-A^2. The singleton first sector is index0, the entire middle\nsector is I, the singleton last queried sector is indexR, and n is the\nactual unqueried terminal. In particular h_K has dyadic level1, not0:\nK+1=2^m-2 is divisible by2 but not4. Every physical leaf remains.\n\nNormalize a legal instance to L=1, f_star=0, x_star=g_star=0. Write\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and use exactly the accepted orientation\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)\n\nThe objective-certificate level is denoted chi, to avoid confusion with\nany gradient or benchmark capacity. It would bound 2f_n/||x_0||^2.\nGeneral L follows by dividing the objective by L and translating its own\nspecified minimizer; the normalization stays W=2R.\n\nElementary bounds used below are jointly recorded here. Since\nlambda<5/2 and epsilon_h<=1/6144, in fact c<5/2; for example use\nsqrt(2)<10/7, lambda<17/7, and (17/7)(6145/6144)<5/2.\nAlso beta_h>99/100, so\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2, 0<e<1, 7/8<w<1.             (1.2)\n\nThe upper bound on a follows from a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.\nFor the lower bound use a>(99/100)/(5/2)=99/250>1/3.\nThere are n-3 ordinary middle entries, all exceeding1, so v>n-2>5.\nIn particular U>6 and D>18. Much weaker horizon bounds would suffice.\n\nFor later mass comparisons put X_h,j=1+sum H_j. Literal summation gives\nX_h,(j+1)=2X_h,j+a*c^j, X_h,0=1, hence\n\n    X_h,j=(1-v_h)2^j+v_h*c^j, v_h=a/(c-2).           (1.3)\n\nSubstitution verifies this symbolic formula, without evaluating a\nrecurrence. Since c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_h, one has 0<v_h<1.\nConsequently X_h,m<c^m, S<c^m, and B<c^m.\n\n## 2. A concrete three-sector row identity, rederived jointly\n\nStart by imposing g_i=q for i in I, and put p=g_0, r=g_R. Define\n\n    alpha=A/(2D), theta=(U+e)/(2D-U),\n    beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta,\n    ell=alpha, eta=alpha/(d+v), R_flow=alpha-ell=0,\n    kappa=beta+ell+eta=theta+eta,\n    gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r.                    (2.1)\n\nUse these core weights mu on ORIGINAL rows:\n\n| Row | mu |\n|---|---:|\n| Q_(0,star) | alpha |\n| Q_(K,star) | beta |\n| Q_(R,star) | gamma |\n| Q_(K,0) | ell+eta |\n| Q_(0,K) | eta |\n| Q_(R,K) | beta+ell |\n| Q_(R,0) | R_flow=0 |\n\nAlso put weight1 on Q_(n,R), the actual last-step row. All unlisted\nweights are zero. It is convenient to retain the symbol R_flow in\nsome algebra so that its role as a row coefficient is explicit.\n\nThese weights are nonnegative. Indeed\n\n    theta=(U+e)/(U+2e+2w) <1,\n    theta>1/2 because U>2w,\n    alpha<1/8, beta>3/8, gamma>0, eta>0, kappa>1/2. (2.2)\n\nThe value coefficient at each of0 and K is zero; at R it is1.\nThe last-step row moves this coefficient to n. Thus all values cancel\nin chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu Q, without assuming any formula for them.\nThe value balance will remain valid when the gradients are released.\n\nThe last row is the exact identity\n\n    Q_(n,R)=f_n-f_R+(A-A^2/2)||r||^2\n                            +||g_n+(A-1)r||^2/2.    (2.3)\n\nThis keeps the terminal free; its minimizing formal gradient is not\nsilently imposed on a legal function. It also explains the charge w.\n\nDirect expansion on the equal-middle subspace gives\n\n    K_0=(alpha-eta*d)||p||^2+kappa*v||q||^2+w||r||^2\n       +[beta*A+ell*d+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>\n       +[gamma*A+R_flow*d]<p,r>\n       +[B-(beta+ell)*v]<q,r>.                    (2.4)\n\nThe polynomial after value cancellation is\n\n    chi||x_0||^2-2<gbar,x_0>+2K_0\n                                      +||g_n+(A-1)r||^2.\n\nThe two identities\n\n    alpha*(2D-1)=A-eta*(d+v),\n    theta*(2D-U)=U+e                             (2.5)\n\nfollow by substituting (2.1). Expanding K_0-D||gbar||^2 in\ninc_pq=p-q and inc_qr=q-r, its common-gradient terms vanish by (2.5), and\n\n    K_0-D||gbar||^2=J11||inc_pq||^2+J22||inc_qr||^2,\n    J11=alpha-eta*d-D*alpha^2\n       =alpha*[v-d*(1+v+d)]/[2(d+v)],\n    J22=theta*(U-D*theta).                       (2.6)\n\nFor completeness the possible mixed entry is\ngamma*(2D*alpha-A)/2=0, which follows from the first definition\nin (2.1). Thus this is a whole two-increment identity, not a diagonal\ntest with an omitted mixed coefficient.\n\nBoth entries are positive. The numerator in J11 is\n(1-d)v-d(1+d)>v/2-3/4>0. For J22, expansion gives\n\n    U*(2D-U)-D*(U+e)=U*w-e*(e+w)>0,\n\nbecause U>6,w>7/8,e<1,w<1. The denominator 2D-U is positive.\n\nSet chi0=1/(2D)=1/(Z-A^2). The complete equal-middle identity is\n\n    chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu Q\n      =(chi-chi0)||x_0||^2\n       +2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2\n       +2J11||p-q||^2+2J22||q-r||^2\n       +||g_n+(A-1)r||^2.                        (2.7)\n\nThe allowed chi_target=17/(16Z) is greater than chi0 because\nZ>17A^2. This follows from A<3/2 and Z>2n+1, n=2^m-1.\nThus there really is nonnegative objective slack available. The failures\nbelow take x_0=0 and therefore survive even arbitrarily large chi.\n\n## 3. Mechanism1a: exact lift with the actual last middle anchor\n\nRelease every middle gradient, keep q=g_K, and define only algebraically\n\n    E=sum_(i=1)^(K-1)h_i*(g_i-q).                 (3.1)\n\nThen\n\n    x_K=x_0-Ap-Mq-E,\n    x_R=x_0-Ap-Bq-E.\n\nKeep exactly the row weights of Section2. No interpolation row is placed\nat a mean. Relative to (2.4), the displacement terms of the affected\nrows are as follows:\n\n| Row | additional term |\n|---|---|\n| Q_(K,star) | <q,E> |\n| Q_(R,star) | <r,E> |\n| Q_(K,0) | <q,E> |\n| Q_(0,K) | -<p,E> |\n| Q_(R,K) | 0 |\n| Q_(R,0) | <r,E> |\n| Q_(0,star), Q_(n,R) | 0 |\n\nEvery f_i is still independent before cancellation. In particular there\nis no hidden equality f_K=f_1-M||q||^2. The coefficients of f_0,f_K,f_R\ncancel by the literal balance in Section2; all other intermediate\nsample values have coefficient0, not an invented value at a mean.\n\nConsequently (2.7) holds on the ENTIRE original formal space after adding\n\n    2<E,C>,\n    C=-eta*p+kappa*q+(gamma+R_flow)*r.            (3.2)\n\nThis is the exact frozen-weight remainder. Every internal gradient is\npresent in E, including g_1; the last middle endpoint remains q=g_K.\nThere is no positive ||E||^2 term.\n\nThis frozen lift is not PSD for any finite chi. Choose a unit vector z,\nx_0=0, p=q=r=z and g_n=-(A-1)z. Choose one actual internal i<K and\nset g_i=q-T*z/h_i, keeping the other middle gradients q. Then E=-Tz,\nC=z because -eta+kappa+gamma+R_flow=1, and the entire certificate is\n\n                         2D-2T.\n\nIt is negative whenever T>D. This is a FORMAL direction only: it is not\nclaimed to satisfy the interpolation inequalities. Its purpose is the\nfull-space PSD test required of a dual certificate. The available\nobjective slack cannot repair it because x_0=0.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1b: a legal fan of actual rows, with arbitrary probabilities\n\nA natural attempt to create internal squares is to replace the q-node\nrows by averages of ACTUAL query rows. Fix any probabilities omega_i>=0\nfor i in I, with sum omega_i=1. Retain alpha,beta,gamma,ell,eta,R_flow\nfrom Section2. Use the following original weights:\n\n    mu_(0,star)=alpha, mu_(R,star)=gamma,\n    mu_(i,star)=beta*omega_i,\n    mu_(i,0)=(ell+eta)*omega_i,\n    mu_(0,i)=eta*omega_i,\n    mu_(R,i)=(beta+ell)*omega_i          (i in I),\n    mu_(R,0)=R_flow,\n    mu_(n,R)=1.                                  (4.1)\n\nAll are explicit nonnegative original-row multipliers. There are no\nmiddle-to-middle rows in this particular fan. The balance at EVERY\nmiddle index is\n\n    beta*omega_i+(ell+eta)*omega_i\n                         -eta*omega_i-(beta+ell)*omega_i=0.\n\nThus all original sampled values cancel separately. No averaged value\nor fictitious jet is introduced. Define algebraic moments\n\n    P_i=sum_(l=1)^(i-1)h_l*g_l,  P_1=0,\n    dvec=sum_(i in I)h_i*g_i,\n    q_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*g_i,\n    N_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*||g_i||^2,\n    P_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*P_i,\n    T_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*<g_i,P_i>,\n    gbar_omega=alpha*p+beta*q_omega+gamma*r.         (4.2)\n\nThe term T_omega is chronologically ordered. It is not the square of a\nmean. Expanding every row in (4.1), using x_i=x_0-Ap-P_i and\nx_R=x_0-Ap-dvec, gives exactly\n\n    chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu Q\n      =chi||x_0||^2-2<gbar_omega,x_0>+2K_omega\n                                        +||g_n+(A-1)r||^2,       (4.3)\n\nwhere\n\n    K_omega=(alpha-eta*d)||p||^2+kappa*N_omega+w||r||^2\n       +[beta*A+ell*d+eta*(d-1)]<p,q_omega>\n       +[gamma*A+R_flow*d]<p,r>\n       +<r,dvec>-eta*<p,P_omega>\n       -(beta+ell)*<r,P_omega+q_omega>+kappa*T_omega.             (4.4)\n\nOne can check every coefficient directly as follows. The combined\ncoefficient of ||g_i||^2 is\n[beta+(ell+eta)+eta+(beta+ell)]omega_i/2=kappa*omega_i.\nThe coefficient of <g_i,P_i> is\n[beta+(ell+eta)]omega_i=kappa*omega_i.\nThe reverse rows Q_(0,i) contribute -eta<p,P_omega>; the Q_(R,i)\nrows contribute -(beta+ell)<r,P_omega+q_omega>. All rows involving\nx_R contribute exactly <r,dvec>, because\ngamma+beta+ell+R_flow=1. These exhaust internal occurrences.\n\nWhen omega is the point mass at K, q_omega=g_K and (4.4) reduces to\nSection3 including its displacement remainder. This independently\nchecks the actual endpoint orientation.\n\n## 5. Exact displacement-mean variance and chronological remainder\n\nNow use the concrete probabilities\n\n    omega_i=h_i/B,\n    q=q_omega=dvec/B,\n    nu_i=g_i-q, sum_(i in I)h_i*nu_i=0.            (5.1)\n\nThe symbol q here denotes only an algebraic displacement mean. The\nlegal proof expression is the original row sum (4.1), not an\ninterpolation row for q. Define\n\n    H2=sum_(i in I)h_i^2,\n    tau=(B^2-H2)/(2B), Delta_tau=tau-M,\n    Cum_i=sum_(l=1)^i h_l,\n    Z_chron=(1/B)*sum_(i in I)h_i*(B-Cum_i)*nu_i,\n    J2=sum_(i in I)h_i^2*nu_i,\n    V=sum_(i in I)h_i*||nu_i||^2.                 (5.2)\n\nThe exact moment identities are\n\n    N_omega=||q||^2+V/B,\n    P_omega=tau*q+Z_chron,\n    T_omega=tau*||q||^2-<q,J2>/B\n                         -sum_i h_i^2||nu_i||^2/(2B).           (5.3)\n\nProof: swap the finite sums defining P_omega to obtain its coefficient\nh_i(B-Cum_i)/B. For T_omega, each unordered pair appears once with\nweight h_i*h_l/B, so\n\n    T_omega=[||sum_i h_i*g_i||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2]/(2B).\n\nExpand g_i=q+nu_i and use the weighted zero-sum equation. This proves\n(5.3), including the term linear in J2; that term cannot be discarded.\n\nLet K_0(p,q,r) be the polynomial (2.4), evaluated algebraically at this\nmean q. Substitution in (4.4) gives the COMPLETE remainder\n\n    K_omega=K_0(p,q,r)+R_var,\n\n    R_var=Delta_tau*[kappa||q||^2-eta<p,q>-(beta+ell)<r,q>]\n       -eta<p,Z_chron>-(beta+ell)<r,Z_chron>\n       -(kappa/B)<q,J2>\n       +(kappa/(2B))*sum_i h_i*(2-h_i)*||nu_i||^2.                (5.4)\n\nThus the complete expression (4.3) is precisely (2.7), with q now\nthe algebraic mean, plus 2R_var. Every formal gradient, x_0 and the\nfree g_n remains present. No boundary square or common-gradient\ncharge is duplicated. The four types of lost terms are explicit:\n\n1. Delta_tau changes the sampled middle endpoint from physical mass M\n   to the average chronological location tau.\n2. Z_chron records the chronological order of every internal variation.\n3. J2 records the cross term between the mean and squared step weights.\n4. The remaining diagonal variance has signed weights h_i(2-h_i).\n\nThe first middle point is included with P_1=0; the last middle point is\nincluded with its actual h_K and prefix mass M. Its contribution to\nZ_chron has B-Cum_K=0. Original values disappear by separate balance,\nas shown before any mean was introduced. These observations account\nfor the endpoints and sampled values that a collapsed argument loses.\n\n## 6. Exact full-space obstruction to displacement-weighted row averaging\n\nSet x_0=p=r=g_n=0. In (4.3), before any mean substitution, the complete\ninternal principal form is\n\n    2*kappa*[sum_i omega_i||g_i||^2\n                         +sum_(l<i)omega_i*h_l<g_i,g_l>].       (6.1)\n\nFor omega_i=h_i/B, this is\n\n    (kappa/B)*[||sum_i h_i*g_i||^2\n                              +sum_i h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2].      (6.2)\n\nBoth formulas follow directly from (4.4) and do not assume any common\ngradient contact equations. In particular, the following obstruction\nalso applies to any outer coefficients of the same fan architecture\nwith kappa>0, whenever the displayed node balances hold.\n\nThere are two actual interior steps with h_i,h_j>2: choose the whole\nroot i=2^(m-1)-1 and the right-child root j=3*2^(m-2)-1. Both are in I,\nand h_i=1+a*c^(m-1), h_j=1+a*c^(m-2). Bounds (1.2), c>2 and m>=1536\ngive the strict inequalities h_i,h_j>2 without any numeric evaluation.\n\nFor a nonzero vector z set g_i=z/h_i, g_j=-z/h_j and all other middle\ngradients zero. Then sum h_l*g_l=0, and (6.2) equals\n\n    (2*kappa/B)*(1/h_i+1/h_j-1)*||z||^2 <0.        (6.3)\n\nThis is an exact full-space negative direction at every diagnostic m.\nNo x_0 allowance, including 17/(16Z), changes it. A merely positive\nvariance based on sum h_i||nu_i||^2 is not the actual row remainder.\nThe chronological part contributes the negative h_i^2 weights.\n\nFor arbitrary positive omega, a two-coordinate principal minor of\n(6.1), at l<i, also yields the necessary condition\n\n    omega_l >= (h_l^2/4)*omega_i.                 (6.4)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries, after removing the common positive factor,\nare omega_l,omega_i and its off-diagonal is h_l*omega_i/2. At the\ndisplacement probabilities this condition becomes h_l*h_i<=4, which\nfails for the two selected long steps. Non-displacement probabilities\nare NOT ruled out by this argument, and (6.4) is not sufficient for\ntheir full-space PSD. No adaptive probability family is asserted.\n\n## 7. Mechanism2: a concrete four-sector discriminator on the same word\n\nLet J=2^(m-1)-1 be the physical whole root, and split the actual middle\nqueries into I_L={1,...,J} and I_R={J+1,...,K}. Put\n\n    B_L=sum_(i in I_L)h_i, B_R=sum_(i in I_R)h_i, B=B_L+B_R,\n    V_L=sum_(i in I_L)h_i(2-h_i),\n    V_R=sum_(i in I_R)h_i(2-h_i).                 (7.1)\n\nThe four queried gradients are p at0, s on I_L, t on I_R, r atR.\nIts three genuine change locations are0,J,K. This discriminator is\nselected by the failed middle lift; it is not another proof of an\narbitrary checkpoint-pair subclass.\n\nBoth V_L and V_R are strictly less than -c^m. To prove this, note that\ntheir total positive linear contribution is at most2S<2c^m. I_L\ncontains the whole root, whose square exceeds c^(2m-2)/9 by a>1/3.\nI_R contains the right-child root, whose square exceeds c^(2m-4)/9.\nFor m>=9, c>2 gives c^(m-4)>2^5>27, and hence each of these root\nsquares exceeds3c^m. Subtracting even just that square proves\n\n    V_L<-c^m<-B, V_R<-c^m<-B.                   (7.2)\n\nTake a nonzero z and the formal assignment\n\n    p=r=x_0=g_n=0,\n    s=z/B_L, t=-z/B_R.                          (7.3)\n\nAll three queried changes are nonzero. Its full middle displacement\nis zero. On this FOUR-sector subspace the averaged certificate has\nthe strictly negative value\n\n    (kappa/B)*[V_L/B_L^2+V_R/B_R^2]*||z||^2.      (7.4)\n\nThus the obstruction is not dependent on isolated gradient spikes or\nan unbounded number of distinct queried gradients.\n\nCrucially, (7.3) is NOT a legal primal escape. The actual first step is\nx_1=x_0-Ap. The two original rows between0 and1 add to\n\n    Q_(0,1)+Q_(1,0)\n       =-d||p||^2+(d-1)<p,s>+||s||^2.           (7.5)\n\nAt (7.3) this is ||s||^2>0, contradicting interpolation. Equivalently,\nfor any legal first change, (7.5)<=0 is\n\n    ||s-(1-A/2)p||^2 <= (A^2/4)||p||^2,          (7.6)\n\nso p=0 forces s=0. The negative direction is excluded by a specific\noriginal omitted inequality. Moreover r=0 on any legal transcript\nwould mean the last query and terminal are minimizers, giving zero\nterminal objective. There is no asserted static function for (7.3).\n\n## 8. The single first-boundary-cycle repair also fails, with its price paid\n\nTest the concrete repair obtained by adding a nonnegative weight zeta\nto EACH of Q_(0,1) and Q_(1,0) in the mu convention. This leaves all\nvalues balanced and adds 2*zeta times (7.5) to the certificate. It is\nthe most direct actual-row repair of the violating pair just found.\nAll original fan weights are otherwise unchanged.\n\nOn (7.3), nonnegativity requires\n\n    zeta >= (kappa/(2B))*(-V_L-(B_L^2/B_R^2)*V_R)\n           > kappa/2 >1/4,                     (8.1)\n\nusing (7.2) and kappa>1/2.\n\nNow test a different formal vector in the SAME four-sector space:\nx_0=0, p nonzero, s=t=r=g_n=0. Formula (4.4) gives the fan value\n2*(alpha-eta*d)||p||^2; (7.5) costs -d||p||^2. Nonnegativity requires\n\n    zeta <= (alpha-eta*d)/d < alpha/d\n           =A/(2D*d) <9/(4D)<1/8,               (8.2)\n\nbecause A<3/2,d>1/3,D>18. This contradicts (8.1).\n\nTherefore NO zeta>=0 makes this repaired fan PSD, already on this\nfour-sector subspace, for ANY objective level chi. The initial\nnegative charge in the cycle is essential; treating it as a free\npositive ||s-p||^2 would conceal exactly this contradiction.\n\nThis is a scoped failure of one paid repair, not a proof that all\ncycles, all fan probability choices, or all original multipliers fail.\nAdding other rows could alter both tests. Such a new full-space\nconstruction has not been derived in this invocation.\n\n## 9. Complete original endpoint system for this four-sector transcript\n\nTo make the legality test explicit, here is its ENTIRE endpoint\ndescription, including all remote rows and the free terminal. Use\nsector labels a_s=0,1,2,3, with vectors\n\n    G_0=p, G_1=s, G_2=t, G_3=r,\n    L_0=A, L_1=B_L, L_2=B_R, L_3=A,\n    X_0=x_0, X_(a_s+1)=X_a_s-L_a_s*G_a_s.\n\nThe actual terminal position is x_n=X_4. Define the within-query\nendpoint masses\n\n    Tcap_0=0,\n    Tcap_1=B_L-h_J,\n    Tcap_2=B_R-h_K,\n    Tcap_3=0.                                   (9.1)\n\nLet F_a_s denote the value at the first point of sector a_s. At any\nactual queried cumulative mass tau_s in that sector the data are\n\n    x=X_a_s-tau_s*G_a_s,\n    g=G_a_s,\n    f=F_a_s-tau_s*||G_a_s||^2.                  (9.2)\n\nFor a legal equal-gradient sector, the two original interpolation\ndirections force exactly this value formula. For a proposed transcript,\n(9.2) defines all physical samples; no steps are merged or removed.\n\nFor different sectors a_s,b_s, define\n\n    C_(a_s,b_s)=F_a_s-F_b_s\n       +<G_a_s,X_b_s-X_a_s>+||G_a_s-G_b_s||^2/2,\n    D_(a_s,b_s)=||G_b_s||^2-<G_a_s,G_b_s>.\n\nThe exact row from any source mass tau_s to a target mass sigma_s is\n\n    Q=C_(a_s,b_s)+sigma_s*D_(a_s,b_s). Since the target dependence is affine and\nboth endpoint masses0 and Tcap_b_s occur physically, ALL ordered rows\nbetween those sectors are equivalent to the two conditions\n\n    C_(a_s,b_s)<=0,\n    C_(a_s,b_s)+Tcap_b_s*D_(a_s,b_s)<=0          (9.4)\n\nfor every ordered pair a_s!=b_s. (9.5)\n\nIndeed the second orientation is increasing in the actual mass.\n\nLet the free terminal gradient and value be z_n,f_n. (9.7)\n\nFinally the two terminal-optimum rows are\n\n    f_n-<z_n,X_4>+||z_n||^2/2<=0,\n    -f_n+||z_n||^2/2<=0. They can be checked by (9.3)\non every physical sample. No feasibility or favorable objective is\nasserted merely from writing this finite system.\n\nFor context, if vectors and values DID satisfy the entire system,\naccepted1252 supplies a single static interpolant using all original\njets. With delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2 and y_j=x_j-g_j, including the zero\noptimum jet, it is\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_j[<y_j,v>-delta_j],\n    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-H(v)].                     (9.9)\n\nThe optimum rows ensure delta_j>=0. Thus H(0)=0, the active affine\npieces at0 are exactly those with delta_j=0, and its complete minimum\nset is\n\n    argmin F=partial H(0)=conv{y_j:delta_j=0}.    (9.10)\n\nThis last equality follows directly from the directional derivative of\na finite maximum at0, together with the zero derivative of ||v||^2/2;\nx is a minimizer of F exactly when0 maximizes <x,v>-H(v), equivalently\nx is a subgradient of H at0. The set includes0 from the optimum jet.\nIts true initial squared radius would be\n\n    dist(x_0,conv{y_j:delta_j=0})^2.             (9.11)\n\nThese formulas are an exact conditional completion/radius interface,\nNOT an initialized adversary or a radius value for (7.3). That formal\ndirection already fails (9.4) at the first boundary, so applying the\ninterpolant construction to it is unjustified.\n\n## 10. What a successful next lift must actually supply\n\nThe frozen and mean-weighted identities locate the missing terms rather\nthan suppressing them. Neither a diagonal-variance claim nor a choice\nof the available chi slack pays them.\n\nA new proof must give nonnegative original weights with exact value\nbalance and a full-space bound. It could change the middle probability\nlaw, add actual middle-to-middle rows, or recursively distribute flow,\nbut none of those mechanisms is completed here. For a fan with a\ndifferent probability law, even its internal two-coordinate blocks\nmust satisfy (6.4), and the chronological vectors P_omega,T_omega and\nthe independent displacement dvec still remain in (4.4). A successful\nrecursion cannot keep only the displacement mean while forgetting\nthese moments. Its boundary state and sign closure must be explicit.\n\nThe two formal tests in Section8 show why the first missing adjacent\ncycle cannot simply be assigned whatever weight repairs (7.4): its\ninitial charge must also be paid. They do not exclude a larger network\nthat supplies that price through other rows. No unspecified PSD block\nis offered as a theorem, and no unknown block has been assigned a\npositive sign.\n\nNo new substantive mechanism is begun after these two permitted ones.\n\n## 11. Literature grounding and retrieval limits\n\nOne Matlas query asked for a smooth-convex, nonconstant-stepsize\ninterpolation certificate with an arbitrary-gradient variance lift.\nIts three results did not provide that interface: their hypotheses\ninvolved a PL condition, stochastic subgradients, or short-step\nself-contractedness. They are not premises.\n\nThe fallback search identified Jason Altschuler and Pablo Parrilo,\n\"Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for\nSmooth Convex Optimization\", arXiv:2309.16530. The official PDF and\nHTML were downloaded under downloads/release_middle_sector/. Theorem\n3.4, its original gluing/rank-one/sparse corrections, Lemmas3.6--3.7,\nand the proof of the gluing theorem were read in extracted official\nHTML text. No theorem from that paper is used as a correctness premise.\nIts exact schedule, coefficients, row orientation and rate invariant\nare not automatically those of this inflated same-m hierarchy.\n\nThe relevant methodological point is limited: that proof keeps internal\nrows and proves the signs of their combined corrections. It does not\njustify replacing actual gradients by a sampled mean. Our obstruction\nconcerns the explicitly written fans (3.2),(4.1), not that gluing\ntheorem or all recursive certificates. No extra literature is needed\nto check the purely algebraic negative directions in those fixed fans.\n\nRetrieval issues were recorded honestly. The first prior-report path\nwas mistyped; the listed correct ancestor_bridge_report.md was then\nread.
