{"id": "9e378dc051b3e416", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T21:29:45.628047+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max85 JOINT UNVERIFIED coefficient-family counterexample: full original PSD, all optimum signs and the COMPLETE first-row total/clock screen do not suffice for finite multiplier nonnegativity. Explicit Law II satisfies those tests but every balanced completion has Hlast<-1/16 and some lambda_(last,j)<0 before the penultimate query. This is not an interpolable primal counterexample.", "evidence": "# Independent common square, positive incoming load, and two row-clock obstructions\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation85.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No new fact or formal submission.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThis report jointly rederives the enlarged common-square architecture and\nall its original interpolation coefficients. The paid zero-sum column is\nzeta, whereas the actual skew divergence is\n\n    d=zeta+(p-s)(v-z).\n\nSeparating z from v changes the architecture, but does not grant free\nsign-feasible transport. The outgoing optimum vector depends on zeta,\nnot on d alone. Both the incoming total/clock moments and the complete\nfirst-row total/clock moments are derived below for GENERAL z.\n\nLaw I uses a clock-zero endpoint common column independent of v, a\nstrictly positive incoming optimum load nu=(1/64)e_0 at the INITIAL\nquery, and an explicitly scaled positive source/last/tail metric. Its full\npolynomial is PSD below the target and all optimum signs pass. Its\nfirst-row total has the required sign. Nevertheless its endpoint clock\ndefect is negative uniformly in every enclosing m>=1536, for every\nskew completion preserving the selected actions. In the ideal endpoint\nmetric, EVERY compatible endpoint incoming load cancels exactly from\nthis defect. The positive load in this law therefore changes individual\nterms without repairing their net obstruction.\n\nLaw II changes terminal probabilities to the actual last queried index\nand adds a specified metric mass1/16 at the penultimate query. It keeps\nthe independent clock-zero common column and the positive incoming\nload1/64 at index0. This changes the failing moment: the full first-row\nTWO-moment screen now passes, as do full PSD and all optimum signs.\nHowever a different invariant, the last queried row tested against the\nPENULTIMATE clock, is strictly less than -1/16. It forces a negative\noriginal finite multiplier from the last query to an earlier-than-\npenultimate query. Every balanced correction annihilating e and t\npreserves this obstruction.\n\nThese are exclusions of TWO fully specified laws, including every\nremaining balanced skew in each law. They are not an impossibility\nresult for the general architecture or for the full dual cone. No\nformal residual vector or negative coefficient is presented as an\ninterpolable primal trajectory. No full-class upper bound on W, and\ntherefore no resolution of the original problem, has been obtained.\n\nThis is ONE joint proof draft. All new calculations are unverified;\nnone is adopted as a separately accepted premise. The only accepted\nfacts used are1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 at the scopes specified\nbelow. The older reports and the main advisory supplied directions only.\n\n## 2. Problem, accepted interfaces, and physical word\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and smoothness L>0,\nW_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original question is whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the original all-split\nbenchmark has\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed residual orientation and\nfull terminal-square certificate interpretation. Only incoming edges\nat the ACTUAL unqueried terminal may be deleted; no queried-prefix\ncoordinate or constraint is removed. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies\nthe word below, its mass formula, and B_n/D<47/50. Only CONDITIONAL on\nthe still-missing full-class W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536 does\nit imply the799/800 separation. That missing hypothesis is not proved.\n\nFix arbitrary integer m>=1536 and keep its parameters in EVERY child:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nThe indices l,r are the last and penultimate QUERIED samples, different\nfrom the actual terminal n. We use the following short local scalar\naliases throughout this report. Their public glossary names carry85:\np=p85, s=s85, qtar=q85, T=T85, U=U85, J=J85, P=P85, H=H85,\nX=X85, r=r85. These explicitly scoped aliases do not change any\nexisting project-glossary definition. The matrix for the chronological\ncoordinate change is Ltri, distinct from scalar T; a superscript T\nmeans transpose. In Section3 only, Len is an integer suffix length.\n\nThe physical last three steps are (b,1+beta,b). Put hgap=1+beta=2-eps.\nElementary bounds from m>=1536 are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.\n\nThe accepted mass formula, also obtained by substitution in\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nConsequently\n\n    2^m<1+S<c^m, c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-20).             (2.1)\n\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queries and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define clocks\n\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b,\n    Delta=T-1=1/p-b-1, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThey are strictly increasing, t_0=1 and t_1=1+b. In particular\n\n    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),\n    0<hgap/Delta<4p<1/128.                            (2.2)\n\n## 3. Tail quantities and the bounds actually needed\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum. For0<=L<=n put\n\n    rtail_L=product_(j=n-L)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n           =beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),\n    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nThe suffix formula follows because h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), and\nsum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=L-sbit(L). With L=l-i one has\n\n    u_i=rtail_L+rtail_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a(1+beta), u_(l-2)=ba beta,\n    U>7/3.                                          (3.1)\n\nFor the last inequality the last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18,\nwhose sum169/72 exceeds7/3. Every rho_i is strictly positive.\nThe following source bound is especially useful:\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.                (3.2)\n\nFor a symbolic proof,2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, starting at16.\nAlso beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.\nThus beta^n<2^(-4m), while b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) yield\nu_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The other inequalities follow.\nNo arithmetic, recurrence, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.\n\nTelescoping the terminal update gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S,                  (3.3)\n\nwhere Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise.\nFor Law I let tbar=sum rho_i t_i and set\n\n    alpha=(1-p)rho, V=t^T(k-alpha)=(1-p)(1/p-tbar).\n\nThe necessary uniform bounds are\n\n    32<V<512m^3, p V^2<1/1024.                       (3.4)\n\nHere is the full derivation, to avoid importing the previous unverified\nreport. The binary product and its digit-weighted sum give\n\n    Ztail=sum_(L=0)^n rtail_L\n         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)\n                    2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)),\n    Mtail=sum_(L=0)^n L rtail_L.\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^(2^j)>=3/4,\nand c+beta^(2^j)<7/2; hence Mtail/Ztail>3m/28. Also ns<1. Therefore\n\n    sum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.\n\nThe suffix after query i has mass at least b+(l-i). As1-p>1/2,\nthe formula for V makes V>32 for m>=1536.\nFor the upper bound, Len=L+1 final steps lie in H_d where d is the least\ninteger with2^d>Len. Thus their mass is smaller than c^d<(2^d)^2<=4Len^2.\nSince u_i<2 beta^L and U>2,\n\n    V<4 sum_(L>=0)(L+1)^2 beta^L\n      =4(1+beta)/(1-beta)^3<512m^3.\n\nThe series identity follows by multiplying by(1-beta)^3.\nFinally2^m>2^30 m^6 for m>=128: the base compares2^128 with2^72,\nand the induction step follows from\n(1+1/m)^6<=sum_(j>=0)(6/m)^j<=64/61<2.\nUsing p<2^(-m) gives pV^2<2^(-12)<1/1024.\n\nLaw II instead chooses alpha=(1-p)e_l. It has\n\n    V=b(1-p), alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0, v_l=a+p,\n    0<V<3/2, pV^2<1/1024.                            (3.5)\n\nIn BOTH laws alpha_star=p, v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s>0,\neta=F/E, e^T v=E, and\n\n    R=Ltri^T v,\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i>0.            (3.6)\n\nIndeed R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i.\nMoreover0<v_0<u_0 in Law I, v_0=u_0 in Law II, and R_0<2p in both.\nFor Law I use alpha_0=((1-p)/U)u_0, with (1-p)/U<3/7.\n\n## 4. FULL original-space identity with an independent common column\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep x_0,g_0,...,g_n\narbitrary and independent, substituting ONLY the actual GD recursion.\nThe accepted residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nFor i<n set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g,\n    g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                (4.1)\n\nLtri is invertible with diagonal1. More explicitly, with\npi_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j),\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<j'<i)(1-h_j')y_j.\n\nThus this change spans ALL original queried-gradient directions.\nThe actual terminal gradient remains independently free.\n\nChoose any z with e^T z=E, positive weights w_i, and zero-sum zeta.\nLet Ww=sum_i w_i and define\n\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww,\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                     (4.2)\n\nThe complete signed multiplier array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.           (4.3)\n\nDiagonal multipliers vanish. In scalar entries,\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(r'>i)(K_(r',j)+A_(r',j))\n                          +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,\n    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)\n\nThe formula defines every original ordered pair; none is hidden behind\nan existence assertion. The finite prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii,\nits incoming total is N_ii-2alpha_i, and the actual terminal adds incoming\n2alpha_i. Hence finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence2,\nand optimum divergence-2. All sampled values cancel in\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nFor prefix nodes including star,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>; the remaining prefix quadratic is\n-g^T N y. Using(4.1) it is\n-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal expansion, with -2f_n\nincluded, is\n||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2-2sum alpha_i delta_i.\nThose delta terms cancel the prefix value terms. Now\n\n    Pe=Ev+Fz,\n    2s v-Pe+d=-2Fz+zeta,\n    eta E^2=p^2-s^2.\n\nSubstitution proves the COMPLETE identity\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n         +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n         +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. As e^T zeta=0, completion gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThis pays the initial charge ONCE. It retains every detail, the common\nsquare and its derivative, and the actual terminal square.\nFull PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J. For necessity take scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), where\nc0=1+[sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i)]/E, and g_n=-s+v^T y.\nEvery displayed square vanishes. Reconstruct the queried gradients by\n(4.1). This is a formal-space test, not a primal interpolation claim.\nIn particular g_n need NOT equal p when z and v differ.\n\nFor the actual common queried-gradient direction g_i=G, y_i=x_0-t_i G.\nPut V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt. The full prefix remainder\nis exactly\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>\n                      +(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.          (4.7)\n\nThe real common derivative theta has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. General optimum moments, complete first-row moments, literal skew\n\nFrom Pe+d=2p v+zeta, the outgoing optimum vector is\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta.                              (5.1)\n\nThe incoming vector is\n\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha.                       (5.2)\n\nSince e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0, its EXACT moments are\n\n    M0=e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    M1=t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.              (5.3)\n\nA nonnegative nu necessarily has M0>=0 and M0<=M1<=T M0.\nConversely the isolated two-moment problem has the nonnegative endpoint\nsolution\n\n    nu_target=((T M0-M1)/Delta)e_0\n                          +((M1-M0)/Delta)e_l.      (5.4)\n\nThis is not sufficient for the finite multiplier signs.\nFor ANY specified compatible nu_target let\n\n    rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.5)\n\nThe two moment identities imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nThus e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplication shows Ae=d and\nAt=rvec, with both terms skew. This is a literal entrywise completion.\nEvery other completion is A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0.\nSuch C leaves the polynomial, mu,nu and all moment tests below unchanged.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Write\n\n    Lcol=Pe-d=2s v+2F z-zeta.\n\nThen N_0j=b Lcol_j-a(P_0j+A_0j), and N_00=b Lcol_0-aP_00.\nAs mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0, the complete general first-row sum is\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bF z_0.          (5.6)\n\nUsing(P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu yields the complete clock identity\n\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0,\n    (Pt)_0=(Kt)_0+Vv_0+eta Zz_0.                    (5.7)\n\nThese formulas explicitly include BOTH optimum directions.\nIf all finite lambda_(0,j)>=0, equivalently N_0j<=0, then necessarily\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                      (5.8)\n\nIndeed b<=t_j-1<=Delta for all j>0. When S0=0 all off-diagonals must\nvanish. These are exact invariant screens, not sufficient row signs.\n\nThere is another invariant at the actual last query l. Its row in\nLtri^T is e_l^T, so N_ll=P_ll, (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\nTesting its earlier entries against t_r, the penultimate clock, gives\n\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n         =2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l-t_r mu_l-hgap*P_ll.  (5.9)\n\nNonpositive N_lj require Hlast>=0, since t_j<=t_r for j<l.\nA negative Hlast forces N_lj>0 at some j<r, not merely at r.\n\n## 6. The shared explicit common column, incoming load, and paid imbalance\n\nBoth laws fix the genuinely independent common column\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, e^T z=E, t^T z=0.          (6.1)\n\nIt differs from v: for example every odd interior v_i is negative and\nnonzero, whereas z_i=0 there. The independence is not a scalar rescaling.\nSet the NONZERO incoming load\n\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.              (6.2)\n\nFor each law's own alpha,V define\n\n    theta0=2(1-p)-2pV=2p alpha^T t,\n    theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-V^2+ellload.                       (6.3)\n\nSelect positive base weights what_i as specified for that law and put\n\n    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what,\n    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/sum w,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                             (6.4)\n\nThis fixes every scale from the actual word before row-sign work.\nIt gives t^T Kt=Xreq, e^T zeta=0 and t^T zeta=theta. Moreover\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).                              (6.5)\n\nIndeed(Kt)_i=w_i(t_i-tbar_w), whose squared inverse-weight sum is\nXreq. Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz shows the same law minimizes this charge\nat fixed theta for its metric. By(5.3), M0=M1=ellload. Thus(6.2) is\nexactly compatible. Define d by(4.2), A by(5.5), then EVERY scalar\nmultiplier by(4.3)--(4.4). The literal law sets C=0; our obstructions\nwill cover every extra skew C annihilating e and t.\n\n### 6.1 Positive metric, full PSD, all optimum signs\n\nFor both laws1<theta0<2. In Law I use V>32 and pV^2<1/1024 to get\npV<1/32768 and then theta0>1. In Law II use V<3/2 and(2.1).\nConsequently\n\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    Xreq<2/p+ellload, 1<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.        (6.6)\n\nThe last follows because theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+pV^2>0.\nEvery base weight is positive because p^2 rho_i>0, and the two unit\nendpoint masses make Vbase>0. Hence all metric weights are positive.\nThe full charge satisfies\n\n    J < [theta0*p/4]*(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      < (4225/8184)p,\n    p^2+J <(17/32)p<qtar.                            (6.7)\n\nFor the second line use p<1/128 and\n4225/8184<67/128, equivalent to540800<548328.\nThus full PSD holds strictly below the requested target for BOTH laws.\nNo interpolation signs are inferred from PSD alone.\n\nThe terminal multipliers are nonnegative, and nu is exactly ellload at\nindex0 and zero elsewhere. All outgoing mu_i are STRICTLY positive.\nTo prove this, zeta_i has the sign of t_i-tbar_w, crossing only once\nfrom negative to positive, and has zero sum. Every proper suffix sum\nis positive: if i is before the crossing, use the negative preceding\nprefix and zero total; if i is after it, every remaining term is\npositive. If zeta_i<0 then sum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, whence\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                     >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the same expression is positive, including i=l because\nzeta_l>0. Equation(5.1) proves all mu_i>0.\n\n## 7. Exact endpoint-clock reduction and incoming-load cancellation\n\nFor (6.1) put Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbar_w-1)>0. Then zeta_0=-theta Y/Xreq.\nWrite\n\n    K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK, Y=Xreq/Delta+eY.\n\nSubstituting(5.6)--(5.7), z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 gives\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[Xreq-T theta-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +a nu_0+2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.1)\n\nThis follows by expanding the metric part as\n a(2Y-Delta K_00)+(a-Delta)zeta_0-btheta,\nand using R_0=bE-a v_0 in the remaining terms.\nFor the endpoint reduction with zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt and ANY\ncompatible endpoint load, not just(6.2), write its total M0\nand moment M1, so theta=theta0+M0 and\nXreq=theta0/p-V^2+M1. Endpoint support means\nM1-T M0=-Delta nu_0. Consequently\n\n    Xreq-T theta=btheta0-V^2-Delta nu_0,\n    (a/Delta)(Xreq-Ttheta)+a nu_0\n                                  =(a/Delta)(btheta0-V^2). (7.2)\n\nThus EVERY endpoint incoming load cancels out of Gendpoint exactly.\nFor our fixed nonzero load, therefore,\n\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.3)\n\nA positive load is not intrinsically useless: it changes other rows,\nthe charge, and the metric perturbation. Equation(7.2) states only this\nprecise endpoint cancellation and does not exclude distributed metrics.\n\n## 8. Law I: tail terminal probabilities, source/last metric, positive load\n\nFix alpha=(1-p)rho and the complete choices(6.1)--(6.4) with\n\n    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+omega*rho.               (8.1)\n\nEquations(4.3)--(4.4),(5.5) specify ALL multipliers. Full PSD and every\noptimum sign already follow jointly from Section6.\nFor exact metric estimates put xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1],\nrbar=sum rho_i xclock_i, and define\n\n    gbase=Vbase/Delta^2,\n    fbase=(1+omega rho_0)(1+omega rbar)/(2+omega).\n\nRetaining the two unit endpoints in the variance minimum gives\ngbase>=1/2; centering at1/2 gives gbase<=1/2+omega/4.\nMoreover1/2-omega/4<=fbase<=1/2+omega and\n\n    K_00/scale=\n       [1+omega+omega^2 rho_0(1-rho_0)]/(2+omega)\n                        in[1/2,1/2+omega/2].\n\nSince Y=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/gbase, these imply\n\n    |eY|<=4Xreq*omega/Delta,\n    |eK|<=2Xreq*omega/Delta^2.                       (8.2)\n\nUse a<1/2, Xreq/Delta<5, theta<3 and0<Delta-a<Delta. The two\nterms of Epert are bounded by25omega and12omega respectively. Thus\n\n    |Epert|<40omega.                                 (8.3)\n\nThe first-row sum passes. More generally the same calculation applies\nto Law II below. Write S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, where\n\n    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.\n\nHere R_0<2p, 0<v_0<=u_0, 0<z_0<2p and eta<1.\nFor Law I K_00/Y<=(1+omega)/Delta<9/(8Delta). In Law II the same\nbound K_00/Y<9/(8Delta) follows from the displayed base weights: the noninitial\nbase mass is at most1+1/16+omega, while its normalized clock sum is\nat least1. Hence, in either case,\n\n    aK_00/Y<3p/4, theta/Xreq>p,\n    Y>1/3,\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16<0.                          (8.4)\n\nFor Y>1/3, in normalized clocks let B=sum what<3,\nM=sum what*xclock>=1, B0=what_0>=1, f=B0 M/B>1/3,\nand H=sum what(xclock-M/B)^2<3/4. Then\nY=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/3 by(6.6),(2.2).\n\nNevertheless G0 is strictly negative in Law I. In(7.3), discard all\nnonpositive terms, use a>1/3, Delta<1/p, theta0<2, Delta>1/(2p),\nand the source bounds to obtain\n\n    Gendpoint<3p-(p/3)V^2+2bsV\n                    +v_0(2apT+2bsDelta)+2a alpha_0\n              <4p-(p/3)V^2.                         (8.5)\n\nIndeed2bsV<3u_0 V<3p/64 because pV<1;\n2apT<1, 2bsDelta<3s/p<1, and2a alpha_0<u_0.\nThus the remaining source contributions are smaller than p.\nBy(8.3), p<2^(-20) and V>32,\n\n    G0 < p[4-V^2/3+40p] < p[5-V^2/3] <0.           (8.6)\n\nThis contradicts the required G0>=0 in(5.8). For EVERY extra skew\nC with Ce=Ct=0, some original finite lambda_(0,j),1<=j<l, is negative.\nThe j=l term has zero coefficient in G0 and cannot create this failure.\nThe nonzero incoming load did not repair the whole-law obstruction.\n\n## 9. Law II: terminal probability at last query and penultimate metric mass\n\nThe second and FINAL concrete law changes BOTH the terminal clock and\nthe interior metric moment:\n\n    alpha=(1-p)e_l, omega=p^2, sigma=1/16,\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho.                 (9.1)\n\nAll other formulas are(6.1)--(6.4),(5.5),(4.3)--(4.4). Thus every\ncoefficient is explicit, with no unknown feasible LP, SDP, or matrix.\nThe selected incoming load is still ellload e_0, genuinely nonzero.\nNow V=b(1-p) replaces the large tail mean from Law I. Full PSD and all\noptimum signs pass by Section6. The first-row sum passes by(8.4).\nWe next show its complete first-row two-moment screen also passes.\n\n### 9.1 The first-row clock changes sign and passes\n\nFirst omit only the tiny omega*rho in an AUXILIARY metric calculation;\nit is restored with a uniform error bound, not removed from the law.\nUse normalized clocks xclock, set rclock=hgap/Delta, Lmass=1+sigma,\nand\n\n    Hnum=Lmass-2sigma rclock+2sigma rclock^2.\n\nFor base masses1 at0,1 at1, and sigma at1-rclock,\nthe scaled variance and source quantities satisfy\n\n    H=Hnum/(2+sigma),\n    f=(Lmass-sigma rclock)/(2+sigma),\n    k=Lmass/(2+sigma),\n    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H, K_00=(Xreq/Delta^2)k/H.\n\nTherefore the metric perturbation from the ideal endpoint expression\nis EXACTLY\n\n    Eint=[sigma*rclock/Hnum]\n       [theta(1-a/Delta)(1-2rclock)-2a Xreq*rclock/Delta]. (9.2)\n\nIn Law II theta>theta0=2(1-p)(1-bp)>31/16.\nAlso a/Delta<1/128, rclock<1/128, Xreq/Delta<5, theta<3.\nThe bracket in(9.2) is greater than7/4: its first term exceeds15/8\nand the magnitude of the subtracted term is less than5/128.\nAs Hnum<17/16 and hgap>15/8,\n\n    Eint>105/(544Delta)>3/(16Delta).                 (9.3)\n\nThe source endpoint term simplifies exactly, not asymptotically:\n\n    btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2=-a^2+s^2T^2.           (9.4)\n\nIndeed the part without s equals\n-[b(1-p)-(1-bp)]^2=-a^2. All terms in(7.3) other than -a^3/Delta, including the\npositive a*s^2*T^2/Delta, have total absolute value less than p^2,\nusing alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0<p^2/64,\nV<3/2, ET<2, pT<1, sDelta<1/64, and Delta<1/p.\nDropping the positive s^2 term thus gives\n\n    Gendpoint>-1/(8Delta)-p^2.                       (9.5)\n\nFor completeness the restored tail weights change the metric expression\nby less than400omega. To see this directly, for any base define\nB=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2, B0=what_0 and\n\n    H=M2-M^2/B, f=B0 M/B, k=B0(1-B0/B).\n\nAdding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Here2<=B<3,\nM<=2, both old and new H>=1/2, and old f,k<=1. Direct quotient\nsubtraction gives |delta f|<=2omega, |delta k|<=4omega,\n|delta H|<=5omega. Hence both ratios f/H,k/H change by at most32omega.\nThe exact metric part of G0 is\n\n    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k/H)\n                  +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.\n\nSince a Xreq/Delta<5/2, theta<3 and0<1-a/Delta<1, its change is less\nthan(240+96)omega<400omega. This comparison keeps Xreq,theta fixed\nand rescales each positive base by its own exact variance, as required.\nThus no hidden scale change is neglected.\nCombining(9.3)--(9.5) and p<2^(-20) proves\n\n    G0>1/(16Delta)-401p^2>p/32>0.                   (9.6)\n\nThe upper clock bound also passes. In(9.2), Hnum>1 and rclock<4p,\nso Eint<3p/4. By(9.4), the absolute endpoint term is less than\n1/(8Delta)+p^2<p/4+p^2. Restoring the tail gives G0<2p.\nTogether with S0<-p/16 and Delta-b>1/(2p),\n\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<2p-1/32<0.                (9.7)\n\nEquations(8.4),(9.6),(9.7) prove ALL inequalities in(5.8).\nThis law truly escapes the first-row moment obstruction of Law I.\nPassing those necessary tests does not establish entrywise row signs.\n\n### 9.2 A different original last-row clock is uniformly impossible\n\nLet xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta as above, B=sum what, and xmean=M/B.\nDefine the positive ratio\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-xmean)/H,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H,\n    (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast,\n    K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,\n    zeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast.                       (9.8)\n\nRetain source-last and source-penultimate terms in the pairwise variance\nidentity H=B^(-1)sum_(i<j)what_i what_j(xclock_i-xclock_j)^2.\nBecause what_l<=1+omega and1-xmean<=(1+sigma*rclock+omega)/B,\n\n    Alast <= (1+omega)(1+sigma*rclock+omega)\n                                  /[1+sigma(1-rclock)^2]\n          <1025/1087<19/20.                         (9.9)\n\nFor the displayed rational bounds use rclock<1/128 and omega<1/16384:\nthe numerator is less than1+1/1024, and the denominator exceeds\n1+(1/16)(1-1/64)=1087/1024. The final strict comparison is20500<20653.\n\nBy(5.9), Z=0, nu_l=0, alpha_l=1-p, v_l=a+p and z_l=-E/Delta,\nthe LAST-ROW defect is EXACTLY\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n           -2+eps*a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2-hgap*eta*z_l^2,\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (9.10)\n\nTo check the nonmetric terms, V-p(T-hgap)=a+p*hgap, and\n\n    2(a+p*hgap)(a+p)-hgap*(a+p)^2-2(1-p)\n                  =-2+(2-hgap)a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2,\n\nwith2-hgap=eps. No row or coordinate was deleted in this calculation.\nMoreover Cclock<2. Subtracting2Delta from its numerator gives exactly\n\n    -ellload/p+2bp+(b+hgap)theta-2V^2+2ellload.       (9.11)\n\nThe positive terms here are less than13, whereas ellload/p>64 by\np<1/4096. Thus the difference is strictly negative. As Alast>0,\nwe may upper-bound the first term of(9.10) by2Alast even if Cclock\nwere negative. Discarding the other nonpositive terms and using(9.9),\n\n    Hlast<19/10-2+eps*a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2\n          <-1/10+5/4096<-1/16.                      (9.12)\n\nHere eps<1/1024, a<1/2, b<3/2, hgap<2, p<1/4096 bound the three\npositive terms by5/4096. But every legal last finite row would require\nHlast>=0. Consequently at least one original\n\n    lambda_(l,j)=-N_lj<0 with0<=j<r=l-1             (9.13)\n\nis forced, for EVERY additional skew C with Ce=Ct=0. Remaining interface and bounded handoff\n\nThe independent common column is a valid enlargement at the algebraic\nlevel; the paid charge is J(zeta), not J(d). The negative parts of s'_1 and s'_2 are\nat most k min(R,1), since their values on the lower edge boundary are\n-k(1+3u)/4 and k(3u-1)/4 and u<=U_R. Also s'_1<=s'_2. The third coordinate\ncontributes at most max(k/2,R-h/2); k/2 is covered by the core term.\nAt the upper ceiling, s'_2 equals\n\n  R/2-h/4+3ku/4,                 u<=2R/3;\n  R-h/4+3(h-2)u/4,              u>=2R/3.           (5.5)\n\nFor h<2 these slopes have opposite signs. The maximum is at u=2R/3\nwhen R<=3/2 and at u=1 when R>=3/2, giving respectively h(2R-1)/4 and\n(R+h-3/2)/2. For h>=2 the maximum is at U_R. For R<=1 it is k(R-1/2),\ndominated by kR; for 1<=R<=3/2 it is R+h/2-3/2, dominated by k; for\nR>=3/2 it is (R+h-3/2)/2.\n\nThere is no vertex region for R<1. For R>=1 its post-step dots are\n\n  (2u+v)/2-h, (v-u+h)/2, -(u+2v)/2+h/2, u>=1.\n\nThe first and third absolute values are bounded by max(k,R-h/2).\nThe only further candidate, the positive second dot, has maximum\nR+h/2-3/2 for 1<=R<=3/2 and (R+h-3/2)/2 for R>=3/2. The former is at\nmost k when h>=2. When h<2 it is at most h(2R-1)/4, because their\ndifference is (2-h)(3/4-R/2)>=0. Negative second dots are covered by the\nfirst-coordinate positive bound. This proves all claimed upper bounds.\n\nFor attainment, the core term is attained by x=min(R,1)v_1. A maximizing\nterm R-h/2 is nonnegative and is attained at the edge-axis point with\nu=0,v=R. In the middle part of (5.2), the edge term is attained at\nu=v=2R/3, which satisfies (5.4). In the outer part, the mixed term is\nattained at u=1,v=R-1/2. For (5.1), if this outer term is strictly larger\nthan k then R>h-1/2>=3/2, so this last point is allowed. If it ties k,\nthe core point suffices. All proposed maximizing cases are thus realized.\n\n## 6. Paid norm and objective conversions\n\nFor every actual x,\n\n  G(x)^2<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.                      (6.1)\n\nFor example, in the chamber u>=v, the difference\n4G^2/3-Q=(u-v)(u+2v)/3 is nonnegative; in the other half chamber\ninterchange u,v. The lower bound follows because each v_i is a unit\nvector. Both constants are attained: on a vertex axis and at u=v.\n\nThe always valid objective estimate is Psi(x)<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.\nFor every R>=3/2 the SHARP objective maximum on C_R is\n\n  max_(G(x)<=R)Psi(x)=2R-1.                        (6.2)\n\nIndeed in a vertex chart Psi<=2R-1. In an edge chart Psi=z-1/4+y^2\nwith z<=R and y^2<=3/4, so Psi<=R+1/2<=2R-1. In the core Psi<=1,\nalso below 2R-1. The point Rv_1 attains equality. Notice that the\nedge-axis point -Rv_1 instead has Psi=R-1/4. This distinction survives\neven for arbitrarily large R; a gauge radius is not an objective value.\n\n## 7. Explicit same-m complete-H_j envelope estimates\n\nWrite E_j(R) for the chronological composition of the EXACT M_h maps\nover H_j, with E_0(R)=R. It is only a scalar enclosing radius. From\nmonotonicity, every actual run satisfies G(T_(H_j)x)<=E_j(G(x)), where\nT_(H_j) denotes the complete actual composition on the one fixed F.\nIt does not follow that E_j is the true image of the whole block.\n\nPut S_j=sum H_j and eta=a/(c-2). Directly from the literal word,\n\n  1+S_j=(1-eta)2^j+eta c^j,    0<eta<1.            (7.1)\n\nFor clarity, A_j=1+S_j obeys A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j; substitution proves\n(7.1), starting at A_0=1. We use the same enclosing m throughout.\nUseful coarse rational bounds are\n\n  2/5<a<5/12, 99/100<beta<1, 12/5<c<5/2,\n  12/5<beta*c<5/2, 1-eta<8epsilon.                 (7.2)\n\nHere lambda>241/100 and lambda<29/12 follow by squaring their positive\nsqrt(2) comparisons. Because epsilon<=1/6144, c<49/20 and beta>99/100,\nso a>99/245>2/5; a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<5/12. Also beta*c=lambda(1-epsilon^2)\n>(241/100)(999/1000)>12/5 and is below lambda<5/2. For the last inequality,\nc(c-2)<1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2 and\n(1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)<1-epsilon give eta>1-8epsilon.\n\nThe exact ruler prefix products are\n\n  P_ell=product_(r=1)^ell(h_(r-1)-1)\n       =beta^ell c^(-sbit(ell)),                   (7.3)\n\nwhere sbit(ell) is the number of binary ones, and P_0=1. The ruler entry\nis beta*c^(nu_2(r)-1); counting each power-of-two divisor of the integers\n1,...,ell gives sum nu_2(r)=ell-sbit(ell), proving (7.3).\nIn particular P_ell<=a<1 for every nonempty prefix and\nP_block,j:=product_(h in H_j)(h-1)=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).\n\nThe following are quantitative complete-word statements, not unevaluated\nmax-plus expressions:\n\n  E_j(R)=P_block,j R,                                  0<=R<=1;\n  E_j(R)<=max(R,a c^(j-1)),                       R>=0,j>=1;\n  E_j(R)<=max(R-S_j/2,C_hex,j),                       R>=0,j>=1;\n  C_hex,j=(3a c^(j-1)-S_(j-1))/2.                    (7.4)\n\nFor the first line, formulas (5.1)--(5.2) give M_h(r)=(h-1)r for\n0<=r<=1 for EVERY original step. For the two short steps this follows\nfrom k>=a>2/5: on 1/2<=r<=1 both r-h/2 and h(2r-1)/4 are at most kr.\nEvery original prefix product is at most1, so no intermediate scalar\nradius leaves this interval. This proves the whole-block equality.\n\nThe other two lines follow from the pointwise bounds\n\n  M_h(R)<=max(R,h-1),\n  M_h(R)<=max(3(h-1)/2,R-h/2),                    (7.5)\n\nvalid for every h>1 and R>=0. To check the second, the mixed outer branch\nis at most3k/2 when R<=2h-3/2 and otherwise at most R-h/2. The short\nmiddle branch is at most the outer mixed branch, since their difference\nis k(R-3/2)/2<=0; the core term is at most k. The first bound follows\ndirectly from (5.1)--(5.2): the mixed outer branch is an average of R and\nk-1/2, and the short middle branch is at most R. The largest k in H_j\nis a c^(j-1), proving the second line of (7.4).\n\nComposing the second max in (7.5) by distributivity gives the upper bound\nmax{R-S_j/2, max_i[3(h_i-1)/2-(mass strictly after i)/2]}.\nAn occurrence of a level-l bridge is followed by its complete H_l right\nchild, so its latter expression is at most (3a c^l-S_l)/2. Equality in\nthis purely affine-max comparison is possible at the last occurrence of\nthat level, whose suffix is exactly H_l. There is no reset of parameters.\nDefine T_l=(3a c^l-S_l)/2. Formula (7.1) gives\n\n  T_l=[1+(3a-eta)c^l-(1-eta)2^l]/2.\n\nThese T_l strictly increase. Indeed 3a-eta=a[3-1/(c-2)]>a/2>1/5,\nand hence (3a-eta)(c-1)>7/25>1-eta by (7.2). Also c^l>=2^l.\nSubtracting adjacent displayed formulas proves T_(l+1)>T_l. The maximum\nis consequently T_(j-1)=C_hex,j, proving the final explicit upper bound.\nIt includes every branch, but C_hex,j grows; it is not a sharp convergence\ntheorem and is not substituted for the desired bound.\n\n## 8. Exact complete-word failure of the scalar relaxation\n\nFor every j>=1 choose the scalar initial radius R_0=S_j+1. At every actual\nposition, let A_i be the original mass already executed. Then\n\n  R_i=S_j+1-A_i/2,\n  M_(h_i)(R_i)=R_i-h_i/2,\n  E_j(S_j+1)=S_j/2+1.                             (8.1)\n\nThis is a symbolic proof for every j, not an executed recurrence. Here is\nwhy EVERY branch choice is valid. Let Hmax=1+a c^(j-1). From (7.1),\n\n  S_j> [c/(c-2)](Hmax-1)-1 >5Hmax-6>4Hmax-5.\n\nThus R_i>=S_j/2+1>2Hmax-3/2>=2h_i-3/2. Also R_i>3/2 since S_j>1.\nIn the outer formulas (5.1)--(5.2), R-h/2 dominates the mixed branch\nexactly when R>=2h-3/2. It also dominates k there, since h>1. This proves\nthe induction in (8.1), including the real last leaf.\n\nCombining the separately sharp conversions (6.1)--(6.2) with this exact\nscalar path gives the certificate value\n\n  [2E_j(R_0)-1]/R_0^2=1/(S_j+1).                  (8.2)\n\nIts ratio to the baseline1/(1+2S_j) is\n(1+2S_j)/(1+S_j)=2-1/(1+S_j). It exceeds17/16 because S_j>1>1/15.\nFor j=m it tends to2 as m tends to infinity, since S_m>=2^m-1.\nThis is a precise, unavoidable loss for the relaxation that keeps only the\nscalar radius, uses these exact one-step images, and applies the sharp\nseparate initial-norm and terminal-objective conversions. Any weaker\none-step bounds or weaker separate conversions cannot repair that loss.\nIt does NOT exclude a bound retaining an objective or directional state.\n\nThe incompatibility is concrete. Start the ACTUAL fixed F at\nx_0=-(S_j+1)v_1. Every point is on the same physical edge-normal ray with\nz_i=S_j+1-A_i/2>1/2 and y_i=0. Its actual gauge is exactly R_i, so ONE\nlegal trajectory attains all the scalar image radii in (8.1). But its\nactual terminal value is S_j/2+3/4, not S_j+1. Its terminal point is on\nan edge axis, whereas the maximizer of (6.2) is on a vertex axis. The\ninitial Euclidean bound is already exact on this edge ray. No orientation\nswitch can be inserted at the terminal of this static trajectory.\n\nAlternatively, the actual start +(S_j+1)v_1 stays in the same vertex\nthrough the terminal and finishes at v_1 with Psi=1. Neither of these\nlegal starts realizes (8.2) as its normalized objective. No full-word\ntriangle counterexample is claimed.\n\n## 9. Actual fixed-face criteria, and why large radius is insufficient\n\nFor a particular vertex, order the other coordinates so its upper gap is\nu_0 and the remaining gap is v_0>=0. The condition\n\n  u_0>=S_m+1                                      (9.1)\n\nis necessary and sufficient for that SAME vertex chart to hold at all\nqueries AND the terminal. Under this condition the gap at any prefix is\nu_0-A_i>=1 and the other gap is unchanged. Conversely, if the chart holds\nthrough the terminal, its affine update gives u_n=u_0-S_m>=1.\n\nFor a fixed physical edge, write x=z n_E+y t_E. The condition\n\n  z_0>=1/2+S_m/2, |y_0|<=sqrt(3)/2                 (9.2)\n\nis necessary and sufficient for that edge chart to be valid throughout,\nincluding terminal. Necessity uses z_n=z_0-S_m/2>=1/2 and initial edge\nmembership. Sufficiency follows from z_i=z_0-A_i/2 and\n|y_i|=P_i|y_0|<=|y_0| using (7.3). Boundary choices are harmless.\n\nBoth fixed-face classes have normalized payoff at most1/D_m. Indeed\nD_m P_block,m^2<1 because (7.1) gives D_m<2c^m and P_block,m=beta^n c^(-m).\nIn a vertex chart the difference between ||x_0||^2 and D_m Psi(x_n)\nis (s_0-D_m)^2+t_0^2. In an edge chart it is\n(z_0-D_m/2)^2+(1-D_m P_block,m^2)y_0^2. The equalities at x_0=D_m v_1 and\nx_0=-(D_m/2)v_1 are legal by (9.1)--(9.2). Thus the classes actually\nattain1/D_m. This is not extended to the face-changing domain.\n\nThere is no scalar large-radius criterion for fixed faces. Choose\n0<delta<a, u_0=1+delta and v_0=T with T arbitrarily large. This is a\nstrict vertex query. The first actual leaf1+a gives ordered gaps\nU=a-delta in (0,1), V=T-a+delta. For T large enough U+2V>1, so the next\nquery is strictly on an edge. Meanwhile ||x_0||^2=u_0^2+u_0 T+T^2 tends\nto infinity. This family rules out assuming that all large starts have\none face. Criteria (9.1)--(9.2), not radius alone, identify the histories\nto which the fixed-face sharpening applies.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"joint": "36b125191170ba08", "original_target": "OPEN", "status": "JOINT UNVERIFIED"}, "glossary": {"E_j": "Composition of exact radius maps M_h over same-m H_j.", "M_h": "Maximum post-step hexagonal gauge on the radius-R hexagon.", "G": "max_i|<x,v_i>| for the specified triangle vertices.", "S_j": "Actual total step mass of H_j, using the same enclosing m.", "P_block,j": "Whole child product beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).", "C_hex,j": "Explicit cap (3a c^(j-1)-S_(j-1))/2."}}

This is NOT a primal trajectory or a full-dual infeasibility claim.", "evidence": "# Clock-balanced terminal pool: endpoint success and a column-capacity obstruction\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation86.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No formal submission or new fact.\n\n## 1. Outcome and scope\n\nThe prescribed new terminal mixture has clock V=2 exactly. Its positive\nmetric, literal skew completion, all terminal and optimum multipliers, and\nfull original-space PSD identity are explicit below. Both complete endpoint\nrow-moment screens pass, uniformly for every enclosing m>=1536:\n\n    first-row S0<-p/16, G0>p/64, P0-b*S0<0;\n    last-row Slast<-1/8, Hlast>1/4000, Jlast<0.\n\nNevertheless the law is impossible, INCLUDING EVERY skew correction C with\nCe=Ct=0. The last queried column has total 2alpha_l, but its invariant\ndiagonal is P_ll. The exact guards give\n\n    alpha_l>19/20, P_ll<1/3,\n    sum_(i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.\n\nConsequently at least one original incoming finite multiplier to the last\nquery is negative. This is a failure of a column mass, not either endpoint\nrow moment, and not a numerical test of the literal completion alone.\n\nThe second mechanism derives a finite transport formulation retaining the\nforbidden diagonal AND the full symmetric-factor equality. Its last-column\ncut excludes an entire family: terminal mass alpha_l>=1/2, the same\nclock-zero endpoint common column, t^T Kt<=3/p, and at least 256p of the\nNONLAST metric weight at clocks <=(T+1)/2. Every positive metric satisfying\nthese conditions fails for every skew completion and every nonnegative\nincoming optimum load. This includes a precisely specified family with any\nnumber of sparse interior metric atoms, arbitrary positive last weight,\nand a positive tail regularizer. No probabilistic existence theorem is\nused or assumed sufficient for the coefficient law.\n\nAll these statements are one jointly derived UNVERIFIED proof draft.\nAccepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used only at their\nstated scopes. Earlier unverified reports supply directions, not premises.\nThis excludes specified coefficient architectures, not the unrestricted\ndual cone, not legal primal trajectories, and not the original minimax\nproblem. It proves no full-class upper bound on W.\n\n## 2. Original problem and accepted interfaces\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nand x_0!=x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original question asks whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the ALL-SPLIT benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed interpolation convention and\nfull terminal-square interpretation. Only incoming edges at the actual\nUNQUERIED terminal n may be deleted. No queried gradient or prefix pair\nis deleted here. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical word and\nmass formula below, and B_n/D<47/50. Its799/800 separation is CONDITIONAL\non the missing full-class bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for EVERY m>=1536.\nThat hypothesis remains unproved.\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536 and use its SAME parameters in every child:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nHere l and r are last and penultimate QUERIED indices. The terminal n\nremains distinct. All short symbols in this report are local aliases;\npublic names for newly published quantities carry the suffix86, avoiding\nany reassignment of project glossary entries. In particular rho below is\na probability VECTOR, not the benchmark exponent. Ltri is a matrix; T is\na scalar clock; superscript T denotes transpose.\n\nLet e be the all-ones column on n queries and e_i its coordinate columns.\nDefine the strictly increasing clocks\n\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1,\n    hgap=1+beta=2-eps, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThe last three physical steps are (b,hgap,b). The needed scalar bounds are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1,\n    2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12, 1-2a>a^2,\n    7/5<b<17/12, 0<eps<=1/6144.                    (2.1)\n\nFor the lower bound on a, sqrt(2)<10/7 gives\n a>7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5; the last inequality is equivalent to\n35*6143>34*6145. The upper bound uses beta/(1+eps)<1 and\nsqrt(2)<17/12. The strict quadratic inequality follows by squaring\n1+a<sqrt(2). Bounds on c follow directly from eps<=1/6144 and these\nrational bounds for sqrt(2).\n\nThe exact mass is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nIt follows either from the cited mass formula or by substituting in\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j. Therefore\n\n    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40),\n    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),\n    dclock:=1/Delta satisfies p<dclock<4p/3,\n    rclock:=hgap/Delta satisfies 0<rclock<4p.        (2.2)\n\nEvery elementary small-p comparison below uses this SAME all-m bound;\nthere is no limit-only argument or evaluated recurrence.\n\n## 3. Tail probabilities and the exact mixed terminal clock\n\nFor integer suffix length Len in[0,n], write sbit(Len) for its binary\ndigit sum and define\n\n    rtail_Len=product_(j=n-Len)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n             =beta^Len*c^(-sbit(Len)),\n    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nHere v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer j.\nThe suffix formula follows from h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j) and\nsum_(j=1)^Len v2(j)=Len-sbit(Len). With Len=l-i,\n\n    u_i=rtail_Len+rtail_(Len+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.\n\nThe last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, whose sum169/72>7/3;\nthus U>7/3 and every rho_i is positive. We will use\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.               (3.1)\n\nFor completeness, 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 follows by induction (the\nbase is the integer inequality at16, and doubling dominates the next\nquadratic). Also beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.\nThen beta^n<2^(-4m). Using b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) gives\nu_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The remaining bounds follow\nfrom U>7/3 and p<2^(-40). All these are symbolic inequalities.\n\nLet Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise. Finite\nproduct telescoping gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t=Ltri*e, t^T k=S.      (3.2)\n\nDefine the two endpoint terminal-clock values\n\n    Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho], Vlast=b(1-p).\n\nWe need Vtail>32, which is rederived here. Put\n\n    Ztail=sum_(Len=0)^n rtail_Len\n         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    Mtail=sum_(Len=0)^n Len*rtail_Len.\n\nExpansion over binary digits gives\n\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)\n                       2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives\nbeta^(2^j)>=3/4 and c+beta^(2^j)<7/2, so Mtail/Ztail>3m/28.\nSince ns<1 by beta^n<2^(-4m) and n<2^m, a direct index shift yields\nsum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.\nIndeed the numerator of this mean is 2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns, its denominator\nis U<2Ztail, and the numerator is positive. The suffix from i through\nthe last step has mass at least b+(l-i). As\nVtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i) and1-p>1/2,\nthis proves Vtail>32 for every m>=1536.\n\nNow set the EXACT proposed law\n\n    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),\n    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT*rho], alpha_star=p,\n    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.\n\nHere Vlast<17/12<2<Vtail. Since b>7/5 and p<1/21,\nVlast>4/3. Hence 2-Vlast<2/3 and\nVtail-Vlast>30, so\n\n    0<xiT<1/45, sum alpha_i=1-p,\n    V:=t^T v=(1-xiT)Vlast+xiT Vtail=2,\n    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,\n    0<v_0<u_0, 0<alpha_0<u_0,\n    e^T v=E, eta in(0,1).                           (3.3)\n\nFor alpha_l use (1-p)(1-xiT)>(1-p)44/45>19/20.\nSince alpha_l<=1-p, v_l>=a+p. Since b<17/12 and alpha_l>19/20,\nv_l<7/15<1/2. At index0, alpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1, proving the\nsource guards. Define\n\n    R=Ltri^T v,\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i\n       =h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.\n\nThe identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (3.2).\nIn particular R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.\n\n## 4. Complete original-space coefficient identity\n\nNormalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep\nx_0,g_0,...,g_n independent formal vectors and substitute ONLY the\nactual GD updates. From fact1252b8823bb5a290 use the signed residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nFor every queried index set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nThen\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                               (4.1)\n\nThe unit-diagonal triangular matrix Ltri is invertible; hence this\ncoordinate change retains every original queried-gradient direction.\nThe actual terminal gradient g_n is independent of these coordinates.\n\nFor ANY positive weights w_i, let Ww=sum_i w_i and\n\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww.\n\nFor any z with e^T z=E and any zeta with e^T zeta=0 define\n\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                    (4.2)\n\nFor distinct original indices the literal signed array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.          (4.3)\n\nDiagonal multipliers vanish. Each matrix entry is explicitly\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                            +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,\n    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)\n\nThus no finite pair is omitted or replaced by a feasibility assumption.\nThe total finite-prefix outgoing mass at i, including its optimum edge,\nis N_ii; the incoming prefix mass is N_ii-2alpha_i; the terminal adds\n2alpha_i. Finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence is2,\nand optimum divergence is-2, even before any sign claim.\n\nFor prefix indices, including star,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>. The remaining prefix quadratic\nis -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal contribution\nWITH -2f_n included is\n\n    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2\n                                         -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.\n\nIts value terms cancel the prefix divergence terms. Since\nPe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2, the full identity is\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n        +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n        +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nWith ybar_w=sum_i w_i y_i/Ww and e^T zeta=0, this becomes\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThe initial charge is paid once. Necessity of q>=p^2+J follows by\nsetting scalar x_0=1, y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i),\nc0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E), and g_n=-s+v^T y;\nthen every square vanishes. Equation (4.1) reconstructs all gradients.\nThus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J, on the entire original formal\nspace, not just on legal trajectories. On the actual common queried\ngradient direction g_i=G, put X=t^T Kt, Z=t^T z and theta=t^T zeta.\nThe entire prefix remainder is\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.\n\nThe common derivative has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. The literal metric, paid column, skew, and all optimum signs\n\nThe fixed common column and incoming optimum load are\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, Z=t^T z=0,\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.\n\nThis z is not proportional to v, since its interior coordinates vanish\nand every odd interior v_i is strictly negative. Choose\n\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM), omega=p^2,\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,\n    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4+ellload=2/p-10+ellload,\n    tbase=sum_i what_i t_i/sum_i what_i,\n    Vbase=sum_i what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                            (5.1)\n\nOne has 0<xiM<1/4 and0<sigma<1/3: positivity follows from\n1-a-a^2/8>1-5/12-25/1152>0, and xiM<a(1-a)<=1/4.\nEvery what_i is positive. The two unit endpoints imply\nVbase>=Delta^2/2 by minimizing their two square terms over the center.\nAlso Xreq>0. Consequently all w_i>0, X=Xreq, e^T zeta=0,\nt^T zeta=theta, and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).\n\nUseful exact bounds are\n\n    1<theta0<2, 2<theta<3,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p, Xreq<2/p,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<8p,\n    theta/Xreq>p.                                  (5.2)\n\nFor the first lower bound on Xreq, theta0/p>1024*4 and\nXreq=theta0/p-4+ellload. For the ratio, subtract2 from\n[2-(10-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]; the numerator difference is\n(2b-8+ellload)p, negative in absolute value less than8p after division\nby1-(b+1)p>3/4. The lower ratio1 follows directly from the same formula.\nFinally theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+4p>0.\n\nGeneral multiplication in (4.2) gives\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.                (5.3)\n\nThe last two identities use e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0. Here\nalpha^T t=1/p-3, V=2 and Z=0, so the two moments in (5.3) are both\nellload, precisely matching nu_target. A literal skew realizing both\nAe=d and this nu is as follows:\n\n    rvec=Kt+2v-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.4)\n\nThe two matching moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nHence e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Direct multiplication proves\nAe=d and At=rvec; each summand is skew. Every other compatible skew\nis A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. Equations (4.3)-(4.4) and (5.4)\ndefine every actual coefficient, with the literal law taking C=0.\n\nAll terminal multipliers are nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers\nare exactly ellload at index0 and zero elsewhere. Every outgoing\noptimum multiplier is strictly positive: zeta_i has the sign of\nt_i-tbase, changes sign only once, and has zero sum. Every proper\nsuffix sum is positive, using either the negative preceding prefix or\nthe strictly positive remaining tail. If zeta_i<0, then\nsum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, giving\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                  >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the expression is positive as well, including i=l.\nTogether with R_i>0 this proves mu_i>0 for all queries.\n\nFull PSD also passes. By (5.2),\n\n    J<(theta0*p/4)(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      <(4225/8184)p.\n\nSince4225/8184<67/128 and p<1/128, one gets\n\n    p^2+J<(17/32)p<17p/[16(2-p)]=qtar.             (5.5)\n\nThus PSD and every optimum sign are valid for this SIGNED coefficient\nlaw. The remaining finite coefficients must still be nonnegative to\nconstitute an upper-bound certificate; Section7 proves they cannot be.\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens pass\n\nAll identities in this section follow from (4.2)-(5.4), not from\nassumed finite multiplier signs. They are unchanged by every C with\nCe=Ct=0.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Since\nPe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta and (P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu, direct multiplication gives\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0.       (6.1)\n\nIf every finite first-row multiplier is nonnegative, then\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0,                      (6.2)\n\nbecause b<=t_j-1<=Delta for j>0. These necessary conditions comprise\nthe complete total/first-moment screen for that row; they are not\nindividual coefficient signs.\n\nPut Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbase-1)>0 and dclock=1/Delta. Normalize clocks by\nxclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1]. For any base what define\n\n    B=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2,\n    B0=what_0, H=M2-M^2/B,\n    f=B0*M/B, k0=B0(1-B0/B).\n\nHere H>=1/2, B<5/2, f>2/5, H<=B/4<5/8, and\n\n    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/2,\n    K_00/Y=dclock*(B-B0)/M.\n\nSince B-B0<3/2 and M>=1, (2.1)-(2.2) give\n aK_00/Y<(5/6)p, while theta/Xreq>p.\nWriting S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, the remaining term is\n\n    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.\n\nFor this use R_0<2p, v_0<u_0<p^2/64, and0<z_0<2p.\nTherefore\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                           (6.3)\n\n### 6.1 First-row clock, with finite errors and the load retained\n\nWrite K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Expansion of (6.1)\nusing R_0=bE-a v_0, z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 yields\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(2-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-4a]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0.           (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in Gendpoint: before cancellation its\nfirst bracket is Xreq-Ttheta-(p^2-s^2)T^2 and there is +a nu_0;\nXreq-Ttheta=btheta0-4-Delta*nu_0. This uses the ACTUAL load ellload,\nnot a zero-load substitution.\n\nAs theta0=2-6p and pT=1-bp,\n\n    btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2\n                  =2b-5-4bp-b^2p^2+s^2T^2.\n\nThe bounds (2.1)-(3.3) now give\n\n    |Gendpoint-a*dclock*(2a-3)|<20p^2.              (6.5)\n\nHere the term a*dclock*(4bp+b^2p^2) is <7p^2; the s^2 term is\n<s^2/p<p^2; 2bs|2-ET|<6s<p^2; the v_0 bracket has absolute value\n<4 and contributes <p^2; aDelta v_0^2<p^2 and2a alpha_0<p^2.\nThe displayed20p^2 safely bounds their sum.\n\nFor an AUXILIARY calculation omit omega*rho only, retaining unit masses\nat0 and1 and mass sigma at1-rclock. Put\n\n    Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\n\nDirect use of the base moments f,k0,H in (6.4) gives the exact interior\nmetric correction\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n      [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                               -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].        (6.6)\n\nThis is an auxiliary formula; the positive tail weights are restored\nbelow. One has 1-2p<Dclock<=1 and\n |1/Dclock-1|<rclock. The bracket differs from theta in absolute value\nby less than35p, using a*dclock<p, rclock<4p, theta<3 andXreq*dclock<2.\nThus\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.       (6.7)\n\nIndeed comparison first with xiM*rclock*theta costs <100p^2, and\nreplacing theta by2+ellload costs <6p^2.\n\nAdding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Both old and\nnew H are >=1/2, and the old f,k0 are <=1. Direct quotient subtraction\nbounds the changes of f,k0,H by2omega,4omega,5omega respectively;\nhence each ratio f/H,k0/H changes by at most32omega. The exact metric\npart of G0 is\n\n    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k0/H)\n                       +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.\n\nUsing a Xreq/Delta<5/2 and theta<3 bounds its change by400omega.\nEach base is rescaled by its OWN variance and the SAME Xreq,theta;\nthere is no neglected change of scale. Combining (6.5)-(6.7),\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<526p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).\n\nSubstitution of xiM gives\n\n    Cfirst=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                      -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n            >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.                   (6.8)\n\nThe last inequality follows from a>2/5, eps<=1/6144:\n4/125-3/24576>1/32. Also |Cfirst|<3. Since p<2^(-40),\n\n    p/64<G0<7p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<7p-1/32<0.               (6.9)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.9) prove the entire screen (6.2).\n\n### 6.2 Last-row clock, including the nonzero incoming-load cost\n\nThe last row of Ltri^T is e_l^T, so\n\n    N_ll=P_ll,\n    (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\n\nFor the last row define\n\n    Slast=sum_(j<l)N_lj=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-1)N_lj=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n                  =(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nNonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0 andHlast>=0.\nDefine actual base ratios\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/H,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H.\n\nThen (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,\nzeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast. Both ratios are positive and <3.\nFor the auxiliary base without omega*rho,\n\n    Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\n\nRestoring omega*rho changes this ratio by at most32omega: its numerator\nchanges by at most3omega, H by5omega, H>=1/2 and the old numerator<=1.\nThus the actual Alast>=1-xiM-32p^2.\n\nUsing V=2, alpha_l=b-v_l, nu_l=0 and z_l=-E/Delta gives the EXACT\nlast-row invariant\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n       +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b-hgap*eta*z_l^2,\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (6.10)\n\nSince T=Delta+1,\n Cclock=2Xreq/Delta-theta+theta*(hgap-1)/Delta.\nBy (5.2),\n\n    2-ellload-20p<Cclock<3,\n    Cclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-40p.\n\nThe subtracted metric term has magnitude <24p, using Blast<3,\nhgap<2, Xreq/Delta<2 and1/Delta<2p. Also\neta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4.\nThe function4v-hgap*v^2+2p(b+hgap)v is increasing for a<=v<=v_l<1/2,\nsince its derivative is >2. Consequently (6.10) implies\n\n    Hlast>2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          >3/8000-65p>1/4000.                      (6.11)\n\nHere the positive eps*a^2 term was dropped. The difference\n(2/5)^3/4-1/64 is3/8000. The load penalty ellload(1-xiM) is therefore\nexplicitly paid; it is not hidden in an asymptotic error.\n\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+zeta_l<19p and P_ll>=v_l^2>a^2>4/25.\nThus Slast<19p-4/25<-1/8. Since (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l-2alpha_l<14,\n\n    Jlast<14-(3/(4p))*(4/25)<0.\n\nEvery last-row total/first-moment condition passes. Neither endpoint\nscreen, even together with all optimum signs and full PSD, guarantees\nnonnegative entries in the full array.\n\n## 7. Decisive column failure of the prescribed law\n\nThe incoming optimum equation in (4.3) is a COLUMN equation:\n\n    sum_i N_il=nu_l+2alpha_l=2alpha_l.\n\nIf each off-diagonal N_il were nonpositive, it would imply\nN_ll>=2alpha_l. But N_ll=P_ll for every skew A, not just (5.4).\nThe metric scale is small:\n\n    scale<=2Xreq/Delta^2<8p,\n    K_ll<=w_l=scale*(1+omega*rho_l)<12p.\n\nTogether with (3.3) and eta*z_l^2<4p^4, this gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta*z_l^2<12p+1/4+4p^4<1/3,\n    P_ll-2alpha_l<1/3-19/10=-47/30<-3/2.            (7.1)\n\nThus the putative nonnegative incoming transport masses have negative\ntotal:\n\n    sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)lambda_(i,l)\n      =sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.\n\nAt least one original finite edge INTO the last queried node has a\nnegative multiplier. This conclusion is invariant under all Ce=Ct=0\ncorrections and, more generally, under any skew with the same incoming\ncolumn sum. The complete prescribed law fails for every m>=1536.\nThis is not a primal counterexample, an infeasibility proof for all\npossible coefficient architectures, or a conclusion about W itself.\n\n## 8. Second mechanism: transport with diagonal exclusion and the full factor\n\nThis section treats an entire family, rather than adding another metric\natom. First give the exact finite transport interface for any fixed\nP,mu,nu,alpha in the architecture above. Write\n\n    D_i=N_ii, a_ij=-N_ij for i!=j, a_ii=0,\n    r_i=D_i-mu_i,\n    c_i=D_i-nu_i-2alpha_i,\n    b_i^clock=t_i D_i-(Nt)_i.                       (8.1)\n\nHere a_ij denotes transport mass and is unrelated to the physical scalar\na. If the original finite multipliers are nonnegative, then a_ij>=0 and\n\n    sum_j a_ij=r_i, sum_i a_ij=c_j,\n    sum_j t_j a_ij=b_i^clock.                       (8.2)\n\nThus every r_i,c_j is nonnegative, sum r=sum c, and\nsum_i b_i^clock=sum_j t_j c_j. A zero r_i forces b_i^clock=0.\nFor positive r_i the mean b_i^clock/r_i lies in the interval spanned\nby {t_j:j!=i}; retaining the exclusion is essential at either endpoint.\nWith Kmass=sum r=sum c, the forbidden diagonal further implies\n\n    r_i+c_i<=Kmass.                                 (8.3)\n\nIndeed all mass from row i must land outside column i, whose total\ncapacity is Kmass-c_i. This is a true cut inequality.\n\nThere are elementary clock cuts as well. For any real tau,\n\n    sum_i max(0,b_i^clock-tau*r_i)\n                              <=sum_j c_j max(0,t_j-tau).       (8.4)\n\nFor each row, max(0,sum_j a_ij(t_j-tau))<=sum_j a_ij max(0,t_j-tau);\nsumming proves (8.4). More generally for any convex function phi on[1,T],\nfinite convexity gives\n\n    sum_(i:r_i>0) r_i phi(b_i^clock/r_i)\n                                      <=sum_j c_j phi(t_j).    (8.5)\n\nThis is merely the finite convex-combination inequality, provable by\ninduction on the number of nonzero row entries, and imports no transport\nexistence theorem. It is necessary, not sufficient for the original law.\nThe following more general elementary cut keeps the missing diagonal\nvisible: if real Ucut_i,Vcut_i,Zcut_j obey\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_j+Zcut_j>=0 for EVERY i!=j, then\n\n    sum_i Ucut_i*r_i+sum_i Vcut_i*b_i^clock+sum_j Zcut_j*c_j>=0. (8.6)\n\nMultiply each allowed coefficient by a_ij>=0 and sum. No condition on\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_i+Zcut_i is imposed, because that edge is forbidden.\n\nCrucially, the chronological symmetric-factor constraint remains\n\n    Ltri^(-T)N+N^T Ltri^(-1)=2P.                    (8.7)\n\nA transport coupling satisfying row/column masses and clock conditions\nalone may violate (8.7). This equality cannot be replaced by PSD of P.\nFor precision, given D and an array a with zero diagonal, nonnegative\noff-diagonals, and the row and column sums in (8.2), define\nN=diag(D)-a. If (8.7) holds, then A=Ltri^(-T)N-P is skew.\nFor the fixed architecture mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta, one has\nAe=Ltri^(-T)mu-Pe=d. Moreover\n(P-A)t=N^T e=nu+2alpha, so At=Pt-2alpha-nu.\nThus this system recovers exactly the required skew and all original\ncoefficients; the clock equations follow with\n\n    Nt=Ltri^T(2Pt-2alpha-nu).\n\nThis is an equivalence of constraint descriptions, NOT an asserted\nfeasible unknown matrix or a new certificate law. We use it only to\nprove the explicit family exclusion below.\n\n### 8.1 Last-column cut and a metric concentration inequality\n\nThe last row of Ltri^(-T) is e_l^T. The (l,l) entry of (8.7) therefore\npins D_l=P_ll. The nonnegative column mass c_l gives\n\n    K_ll+eta*z_l^2 >= 2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2.   (8.8)\n\nThis combines the transport column cut with an exact original-space\nfactor constraint. Allowing D_l to float freely would lose it.\n\nFor ANY strictly positive metric weights w, set X=t^T Kt and\n\n    Qgap=sum_(i<l) w_i (T-t_i)^2 / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThe weighted variance identity is\n\n    X=(1/Ww)sum_(i<j)w_i w_j(t_i-t_j)^2.\n\nRetaining only pairs involving l and using\nK_ll=w_l(Ww-w_l)/Ww gives the exact necessary estimate\n\n    X>=K_ll*Qgap, so K_ll<=X/Qgap.                  (8.9)\n\nEvery denominator is positive since w_i>0 and the clocks are distinct.\nFor d0>0 define the NONLAST far-clock fraction\n\n    Ffar(d0)=sum_(i<l:T-t_i>=d0)w_i / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThen Qgap>=d0^2*Ffar(d0). If\n Bneed:=2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2-eta*z_l^2>0,\n(8.8)-(8.9) imply the quantitative concentration requirement\n\n    Ffar(d0)<=X/[d0^2*Bneed].                       (8.10)\n\nThis is a global statement about ALL nonlast metric weights; it does\nnot assume any sparse support or any special skew completion.\n\n### 8.2 A full family excluded, including arbitrary sparse metric atoms\n\nFix any same-enclosing-m word as in Section2. Consider ALL choices with\n\n (i) alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1-p, alpha_l>=1/2;\n (ii) v=k-alpha, z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, eta=F/E;\n (iii) arbitrary positive w with X=t^T Kt<=3/p;\n (iv) Ffar(Delta/2)>=256p;\n (v) any skew A and any nonnegative incoming optimum vector nu\n     satisfying the original equations (4.2)-(4.3).\n\nThere is no restriction to the literal skew (5.4), to nu=ellload e_0,\nor to the paid minimum-energy zeta. If the divergence and PSD happen\nto pass, they still cannot repair the following finite signs.\n\nFor alpha_l in[1/2,1-p], the function\n2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2 is strictly increasing, because its derivative\nis2+2b-2alpha_l>0. Using b<17/12 gives\n\n    2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2\n                >=1-(b-1/2)^2>23/144.              (8.11)\n\nAlso eta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4<1/144.\nOn the other hand, (8.9) and condition(iv) yield\n\n    Qgap>=64p*Delta^2,\n    K_ll <=(3/p)/(64p*Delta^2)<1/12,                (8.12)\n\nwhere Delta>3/(4p) was used. Therefore\n\n    c_l=P_ll-nu_l-2alpha_l\n       <1/12+1/144-23/144=-5/72<0.                 (8.13)\n\nThis contradicts a nonnegative column mass. Every member of the entire\nprecisely specified family has some negative original multiplier into\nl. No existence theorem, unknown optimizer, asymptotic coefficient law,\nor numerical evaluation enters this exclusion.\n\nAs a concrete sparse-metric subfamily, take ANY finite subset\nJsubset of {1,...,l-1}, arbitrary coefficients Mlast>0 and b_j>=0,\nand omega0>0, with\n\n    what=e_0+Mlast*e_l+sum_(j in Jsubset)b_j e_j+omega0*rho,\n    1+sum_j b_j+omega0 <= 1/(256p).\n\nScale what by ANY positive scalar for which t^T Kt<=3/p. The positive\nregularizer retains every coordinate. The initial atom is far from T,\nand its fraction of the nonlast base weight is at least\n1/(1+sum_j b_j+omega0)>=256p. Hence every such metric lies in the\nexcluded family whenever the terminal mass is >=1/2 and the common\ncolumn is as above. The number and positions of interior atoms and the\nlast weight are unrestricted. The prescribed law of Section5 belongs\nto this family: alpha_l>19/20, Xreq<2/p, Mlast=1, b_r=sigma<1/3,\nomega0=p^2, and1+sigma+p^2<3/2<1/(256p).\n\nEquivalently, even without the coarse256p threshold, (8.10)-(8.11)\nshow that a putative law in this architecture with alpha_l>=1/2 and\nX<=3/p must have Ffar(Delta/2)<140p. Indeed Bneed>11/72 and\nDelta>3/(4p) give4X/(Delta^2 Bneed)<(1536/11)p<140p.\nThis records the quantitative missing interface rather than prescribing\nanother unsupported atom. It does not exclude terminal laws with less\nmass at l, metrics with sufficiently little far-clock mass, another\ncommon column, or architectures outside (4.2).\n\n## 9. Author audit, resources, and handoff\n\nThe audit retains all original queried gradients, all prefix rows, the\nactual free terminal gradient, the terminal square, the independent\ncommon square, its derivative, and the global initial charge. The\nterminal and metric mixtures have separate names xiT andxiM. The\npositive omega*rho weights are never dropped from the actual law;\nauxiliary endpoint formulas include an explicit restoration error.\nThe incoming load penalty in the last-row clock is ellload(1-xiM),\nwhich is necessary for the stated finite margin.
