{"id": "1ec982392e687105", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T22:54:45.589335+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "UNVERIFIED coefficient-level counterexample to sufficiency of full PSD, all optimum signs, repaired last-column capacity, adjacent-pair sign sum and both endpoint row screens: the exact assigned late-common law passes all of those tests while its original first incoming finite column totals less than-1/128 for every compatible skew.", "evidence": "# Late common column and the paid source-column capacity\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation87.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax target OPEN. No fact_submit, no new fact_id.\nGuidance136c8e6e0b9c4945; plan d0d3e2892870802d.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe prescribed late common column genuinely repairs the last-column\nshortage. Its full original-space PSD condition, all optimum signs,\nlast adjacent-pair cut, and both endpoint row-moment screens pass for\nevery SAME enclosing m>=1536. The source column nevertheless fails:\n\n    c_0 := N_00 - nu_0 - 2alpha_0 < -1/128.\n\nHere nu_0=1/64 is the specified incoming optimum multiplier. This is\nthe sum of ALL finite incoming multipliers at the first query, not a\nsign test of one arbitrarily selected skew completion. The entire law\nfails for every compatible skew correction.\n\nThe second mechanism is a whole-class obstruction for the genuinely\nenlarged PSD-detail architecture. Even after changing the detail\nmatrix, its clock energy, the paid column, and the common column,\nfull original-space PSD implies the source capacity bound\n\n    N_00 <= b^2 q/a.\n\nConsequently nonnegative finite incoming coefficients require\n\n    nu_0 + 2alpha_0 <= b^2 q/a.                    (1.1)\n\nFor q<=17/(16D), the right side is <6p<1/128, whereas the prescribed\nnu_0 is1/64. No PSD-detail repair retaining that incoming load can\nsucceed in this architecture. No unspecified PSD matrix is assumed\nfeasible: the second mechanism derives a necessary inequality for\nall members and gives literal chronological detail families it covers.\n\nThis is a restriction on coefficients and their paid initial charge.\nIt is not full-dual infeasibility, not a legal primal counterexample,\nnot a lower bound for W, and not a resolution of the original question.\nThe surviving freedom includes nu_0 of order p or zero, with all other\nloads and individual signs then rederived. No third law is attempted.\n\nEvery new result below belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.\nNo earlier unverified finding is used as an accepted intermediate lemma.\nOnly accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used at their\nstated scopes. The latter's799/800 implication remains conditional.\n\n## 2. Original target, coordinates, and uniform scalar bounds\n\nFor a fixed nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer and x_0!=x_star,\nusing x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original problem is\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ the exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not a decimal profile.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical hierarchy and the scalar\ncomparison B_n/D<47/50, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its separation conclusion\nrequires the still-unproved full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for all m>=1536.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation orientation\nand exact unqueried-terminal reduction. No queried sample is deleted.\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. All children retain these SAME parameters:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll short notation is LOCAL to this report. In particular rho below is\na probability vector, not the benchmark exponent; T is a scalar clock,\nLtri a matrix, and a superscript T means transpose. Public names ending87\nare aliases for this report's definitions, not project-glossary overrides.\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queried indices and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define\n\n    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, Ltri_ij=0 for j>i,\n    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,\n    T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1, hgap=1+beta, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThe last three steps are (b,hgap,b). Elementary bounds are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,\n    1-2a>a^2, 7/5<b<17/12, 15/8<hgap<2,\n    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40), 3/(4p)<Delta<1/p,\n    Delta-b>1/(2p), p<dclock:=1/Delta<4p/3,\n    0<rclock:=hgap/Delta<4p.                       (2.1)\n\nFor the lower a bound use sqrt(2)<10/7 and\n7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5. Its upper bound follows from beta/(1+eps)<1.\nThe quadratic inequality follows from1+a<sqrt(2).\nThe hierarchy mass, also obtained by substitution into\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nThis proves the p bounds; the remaining clock inequalities follow by\nsubstituting Delta=1/p-b-1. These are symbolic all-m inequalities,\nnot evaluated recurrences or asymptotic approximations.\n\nFor integer suffix length Ls in[0,n], let sbit(Ls) be its binary digit\nsum and put\n\n    rtail_Ls=product_(j=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n             =beta^Ls*c^(-sbit(Ls)), s=rtail_n,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j), u_i=|k_i|,\n    U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIndeed h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), where v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j,\nand sum_(j=1)^Ls v2(j)=Ls-sbit(Ls). With Ls=l-i,\n\n    u_i=rtail_Ls+rtail_(Ls+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.\n\nThe last three u terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, so U>7/3.\nAlso 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, and\nbeta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2. Hence beta^n<2^(-4m), and\n\n    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, s<p, rho_i>0.                 (2.2)\n\nFinite product telescoping gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.               (2.3)\n\nFor the terminal pool define\n\n    Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho], Vlast=b(1-p).\n\nHere Vtail>32, as follows without an external estimate. Set\nZtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n rtail_Ls and Mtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n Ls*rtail_Ls.\nExpansion over binary digits gives\n\n    Ztail=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m; Bernoulli's inequality gives its summand\n>3m/28. An index shift gives\nsum_i rho_i(l-i)=(2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns)/U>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2,\nusing ns<1 and positivity of the numerator. Each suffix mass\n1/p-t_i is at least b+l-i. Since\nVtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i), this proves Vtail>32 for m>=1536.\n\nKeep the EXACT assigned mixture\n\n    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),\n    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT rho], alpha_star=p,\n    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.\n\nSince4/3<Vlast<17/12 and Vtail>32,\n\n    0<xiT<1/45, e^T alpha=1-p, V:=t^T v=2,\n    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,\n    0<alpha_0<u_0, 0<v_0<u_0,\n    e^T v=E, 1-p/32<eta<1.                       (2.4)\n\nFor example alpha_l>44(1-p)/45>19/20 and\nv_l=b-alpha_l<17/12-19/20<1/2. At0,\nalpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1. The eta bound uses2s/E<p/32.\nThe vector R=Ltri^T v satisfies\n\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0,\n    R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.                             (2.5)\n\nThe first identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (2.3).\n\n## 3. Full original-space identity and literal coefficients\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the original formal\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n independently. From accepted1252 use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nPut y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 on every queried index.\nThe actual GD substitutions give\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                            (3.1)\n\nThe unit-diagonal triangular Ltri is invertible, so no original\nqueried-gradient direction is removed. The terminal gradient g_n\nis still a separate free vector.\n\nFor positive weights w, define Ww=sum_i w_i and\nK=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww. For e^T z=E and e^T zeta=0 put\n\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T, d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                 (3.2)\n\nThe COMPLETE signed original coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.\n\nAll diagonal lambda entries vanish. For every i,j<n,\n\n    N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj).       (3.3)\n\nThus there is a literal coefficient for EVERY original pair.\nThe prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii and its prefix incoming total\nis N_ii-2alpha_i. The terminal contributes2alpha_i, so the finite-node\nvalue divergences vanish; terminal divergence is2 and optimum is-2.\n\nFor prefix indices including the optimum,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.\nThe prefix quadratic is -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y.\nAfter including -2f_n, the terminal contribution is\n\n    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2\n                                       -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.\n\nThe values cancel. Using Pe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2 gives\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.          (3.4)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. Completing the weighted squares gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+zeta_i*x_0/(2w_i)||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                   (3.5)\n\nNecessity is literal: set scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E),\nand g_n=-s+v^T y. Every displayed square vanishes; (3.1) recovers\nall gradients. Thus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J.\nThe charge is paid ONCE. The common queried-gradient direction g_i=G\nleaves prefix remainder\n(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2,\nwhere X=t^T Kt, theta=t^T zeta, Z=t^T z. It has not been discarded.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: prescribed nonzero-clock common column\n\nSet\n\n    z=b e_l+(E-b)e_r, Z=E*t_r+b*hgap,\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload e_0,\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM),\n    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho,\n    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+ellload,\n    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what_i,\n    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    w=(Xreq/Vbase)what, zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.        (4.1)\n\nThis changes the common column itself. It has e^T z=E, z_0=0,\nz_l=b, and genuinely nonzero clock Z. Put Zstar=1+b*hgap.\nBy (2.1)--(2.4),\n\n    |Z-Zstar|<4p, 3<Z<4,\n    |eta Z^2-Zstar^2|<33p,\n    0<xiM<1/4, 0<sigma<1/3.                      (4.2)\n\nIndeed Z-Zstar=-(b+hgap)p+s*t_r, and0<t_r<1/p.\nThe eta error is at most16p/32; the two squared-clock error is <32p.\nPositivity of xiM follows from1-a-a^2/8>0 and its upper bound from\na(1-a)<=1/4. All what_i are strictly positive.\nThe two unit endpoint weights imply Vbase>=Delta^2/2. Further,\n\n    2/p-26<Xreq<2/p,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<32p,\n    2<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.                     (4.3)\n\nFor the second lower bound subtract at most20 from theta0/p,\nwhich is greater than20480. For the ratio subtract2 from\n[2-(10+eta Z^2-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]. Finally\n\ntheta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+p(4+eta Z^2)>0.\n\nThus e^T zeta=0, t^T Kt=Xreq, t^T zeta=theta and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq), p^2+J<(17/32)p<qtar.        (4.4)\n\nTo justify the strict bound, theta0>1, ellload=1/64 and (4.3) give\nJ<(theta0*p/4)(1+1/64)^2*1024/1023\n <(4225/8184)p<(67/128)p.\nThen use p<1/128 and qtar=17p/[16(2-p)].\n\nFor any skew in (3.2), multiplication gives\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=Xreq+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.           (4.5)\n\nHere V=2, alpha^T t=1/p-3, so both moments equal ellload,\nmatching nu_target. A literal skew completion is\n\n    rvec=Kt+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.             (4.6)\n\nThe two moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nConsequently e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0, and direct multiplication\nproves Ae=d and At=rvec. Each term is skew. All other compatible\ncompletions are A+C with C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0. The displayed law takes C=0;\nthe obstructions below allow all such C.\n\nEvery terminal and incoming optimum sign passes. Every outgoing\noptimum sign also passes. The zeta_i have the signs of t_i-tbase,\nchange sign once, and sum to zero. Every proper suffix sum is positive.\nIf zeta_i<0, the suffix after i is at least -zeta_i, so\n(Ltri^T zeta)_i>= (h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0. If zeta_i>=0 its expression\nzeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j is also positive, including i=l.\nEquation(2.5) then gives mu_i>0 for all queried i.\n\n## 5. Every diagonal/column equation; the decisive first-column failure\n\nFor EVERY queried index define its actual diagonal and finite incoming mass\n\n    Ddiag_i=P_ii+h_i sum_(j>i)(P_ji+A_ji),\n    c_i=Ddiag_i-nu_i-2alpha_i\n       =sum_(j!=i)(-N_ji).                       (5.1)\n\nThese formulas include every query, and (4.6) makes them literal.\nFor another compatible skew C, the diagonal changes by\nh_i sum_(j>i)C_ji. The first and last diagonals, however, are invariant:\n\n    Ddiag_0=b(Pe-d)_0-aP_00,\n    Ddiag_l=P_ll,\n    Pe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta.                          (5.2)\n\nThe first identity uses the exact first row of Ltri^T,\nb e^T-a e_0^T, and skew diagonals are zero. It is NOT a row-sum\napproximation. Since z_0=0 in Mechanism1,\n\n    Ddiag_0=b(2s v_0-zeta_0)-a(K_00+v_0^2).       (5.3)\n\nTo bound it and audit the endpoint rows, normalize clocks by\nxclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta and set\n\n    B=sum what_i, M=sum what_i*xclock_i,\n    M2=sum what_i*xclock_i^2, B0=what_0,\n    Hvar=M2-M^2/B,\n    fbase=B0*M/B, kbase=B0(1-B0/B),\n    Y=-(Kt)_0=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/Hvar.\n\nHere Hvar>=1/2, B<5/2, M>=1, fbase>2/5,\nHvar<=B/4<5/8, and fbase/Hvar<3. The variance upper bound follows\nfrom xclock in[0,1] and xclock^2<=xclock. Hence\n\n    Y>1/2, a K_00/Y=a*dclock*(B-B0)/M<(5/6)p,\n    -zeta_0=(theta/Delta)fbase/Hvar<12p.           (5.4)\n\nTogether with theta/Xreq>p and (2.2)--(2.4), (5.3) yields\n\n    p/4<Ddiag_0<32p,\n    c_0=Ddiag_0-ellload-2alpha_0<-1/128.          (5.5)\n\nFor the lower bound use\nb(-zeta_0)-aK_00>(b-5/6)pY>(17/60)p,\nand a v_0^2 is smaller than p/100. For the upper bound discard\nboth nonpositive square terms and use2bs v_0<p^2.\nThe last inequality uses32p<1/128. Thus the first diagonal itself\nis positive but cannot pay its incoming optimum load.\n\nThis is a uniform FAILED COLUMN of the full array. It rules out\nsimultaneous nonnegativity of the entire collection (5.1), whatever\nthe other diagonals do. It would be false to mark ALL columns as passed.\nSpecifically at least one original lambda_(j,0), 1<=j<=l, is negative.\nNo compatible skew correction changes that conclusion. Having found\nthis cut, there is no need or justification to search individual signs\nof the enormous array. The remaining tests below distinguish which\nof the proposed repairs really work.\n\n### 5.1 Last column really is repaired\n\nThe scale is at most2Xreq/Delta^2<8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p.\nBut the new common column gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,\n    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                      (5.6)\n\nIndeed v_l>a>2/5, eta>1-p/32 and b>7/5 imply\nv_l^2+eta b^2>53/25-(49/800)p>21/10.\nThe incoming optimum mass nu_l is zero. Thus the prior last-column\nobstruction is removed, rather than merely overlooked.\n\n### 5.2 The last adjacent-pair factor cut passes\n\nLet hgap=h_r. Since the last two rows of Ltri^T are e_l^T and\ne_r^T+hgap e_l^T,\n\n    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll.                   (5.7)\n\nThe skew terms cancel exactly. If both off-diagonals are nonpositive,\nthe right side must be nonpositive. For the prescribed law it equals\n\n    2K_lr+hgap K_ll\n     +v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)\n     +eta b[2E-eps*b].                          (5.8)\n\nHere K_lr<0, v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, and v_l<1/2. Thus\nv_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)<-(3/25)hgap<-9/40.\nThe metric term is <24p and the positive part of the common term\nis <4p. Therefore\n\n    2P_lr+hgap P_ll<-1/5.                       (5.9)\n\nThis necessary pair test passes, but does not remedy (5.5).\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens for Mechanism1\n\nThese are row tests, distinct from the column equation (5.5). They\nare included to audit the nonzero-clock change jointly, not as a\nsubstitute for the failed column test.\n\nDefine first-row quantities\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0.\n\nNonpositive first-row off-diagonals require\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                   (6.1)\n\nFrom (3.2), (4.5), and the first row b e^T-a e_0^T,\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                       +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0. (6.2)\n\nFor the new z_0=0, the nonmetric part of S0 is\n2pR_0-2bs v_0+a v_0^2<7p^2. Hence (5.4) gives\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                        (6.3)\n\nFor the clock, put K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and\nY=Xreq/Delta+eY. Direct expansion of (6.2) gives\n\n    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)\n       +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0\n       +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.  (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in this identity because\nXreq-Ttheta=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2-Delta*ellload,\nand the expansion includes the original +a nu_0=a ellload.\nNo incoming load has been suppressed.\n\nFor an auxiliary base omit only omega rho, keeping its three literal\natoms at0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Put\nDclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\nUsing B=2+sigma, M=1+sigma(1-rclock), and\nM2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 in the displayed definitions gives exactly\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n       [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                         -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].              (6.5)\n\nSince1-2p<Dclock<=1, |1/Dclock-1|<rclock,\na*dclock<p, rclock<4p, Xreq*dclock<2 and theta<3,\nthe bracket differs from theta by less than35p. Consequently\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.    (6.6)\n\nRestoring omega rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega.\nBoth variances are >=1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds the\nchanges of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2omega,4omega,5omega, respectively,\nand those of fbase/Hvar,kbase/Hvar by32omega. The exact metric part\nof G0 is\n\na(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)\n       +theta(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-btheta.\n\nIts change is <400p^2. Each base is scaled by its own variance and\nthe same Xreq; hence this includes the change of scale.\n\nIn (6.4), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs\nless than42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2,\nsince |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0\nby-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2; use R_0=bE-a v_0 and pT=1-bp.\nThe five remaining s,v_0,alpha_0 terms total less than5p^2.\nTogether with (6.6) and the restored positive tail, this proves\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)\n                                  +xiM*hgap*(2+ellload)\n          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),\n    Cold=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).             (6.7)\n\nSubstitute xiM and hgap=2-eps to obtain\n\n    Cold=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                  -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n         >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.\n\nHere a>2/5 and eps<=1/6144 give4/125-3/24576>1/32.\nAlso a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and b*hgap>21/8. Thus the extra term\nin (6.7) exceeds21/10, so Cfirst>2. A coarse direct bound gives\n|Cfirst|<20. Therefore\n\n    p<G0<30p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.            (6.8)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.8) establish the entire first-row screen (6.1).\n\nFor the last row put\n\n    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nThese are respectively the row total, its clock relative to1, and its\nclock relative to t_r. Nonpositive off-diagonals require\nSlast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0. Set\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.\n\nThey are positive and <3, with\n(Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast.\nFor the auxiliary three-atom base,\nAlast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\nRestoring omega rho changes this ratio by at most32p^2, by the same\nquotient estimate. Now expansion gives the exact formula\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n          +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b\n          +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.            (6.9)\n\nBy (4.3), Cclock>2-ellload-64p and Cclock<3. Hence\nCclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-100p.\nThe negative metric term has magnitude <24p. The v_l polynomial is\nincreasing for a<=v_l<1/2, because its derivative is greater than2.\nThe terms in (6.9) before the common-column contribution therefore exceed\n\n    2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     >3/8000-125p>1/4000.\n\nThe exact incoming-load loss ellload(1-xiM) is retained.\nSince Z>3, hgap*b<17/6, eta>9/10 and b>7/5, the new common\ncontribution in (6.9) exceeds3. Thus Hlast>3.\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+(theta/Delta)Alast<19p, whereas P_ll>21/10.\nAlso (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l+2eta Zb-2alpha_l<26. It follows that\n\n    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.   (6.10)\n\nBoth endpoint row screens really pass. The first incoming COLUMN,\nnot either endpoint row screen or the last adjacent pair, kills the law.\n\n## 7. Mechanism2: explicit chronological PSD details and their global price\n\nWe now enlarge the detail term itself, instead of changing another\nweight within one weighted-variance metric. The identified constraint\nis the first diagonal's ability to pay a column load under a small\ninitial charge. Both clock-bearing and clock-annihilating details are\nallowed below; the former change the paid column and all load moments.\nTheir price is recomputed, not assumed to vanish.\n\nOn the ORIGINAL queried indices define the literal chronological vectors\n\n    gdetail_i=e_i-e_(i+1), 0<=i<=n-2,\n    ddetail_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2),\n                                           0<=i<=n-3.\n\nThey obey\n\n    e^T gdetail_i=0, t^T gdetail_i=-h_i,\n    e^T ddetail_i=0, t^T ddetail_i=0.              (7.1)\n\nFor the latter clock identity substitute\n(t_i,t_(i+1),t_(i+2))=(t_i,t_i+h_i,t_i+h_i+h_(i+1));\neach term cancels. Thus zero sum alone does NOT imply zero clock energy.\n\nChoose this ONE explicit enlarged coefficient law:\n\n    gamma_i=Xreq/[2(n-1)h_i^2], 0<=i<=n-2,\n    tau_i=p/[1+h_i+h_(i+1)]^2, 0<=i<=n-3,\n    Hclock=sum_(i=0)^(n-2) gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T,\n    Hnull=sum_(i=0)^(n-3) tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T,\n    Qdetail=K/2+Hclock+Hnull.                      (7.2)\n\nEvery vector and weight is an original-index formula. No optimizer,\nrecurrence replay, matrix-existence claim, or selected support is used.\nAll sums are finite symbolic definitions, not executed computations.\nThe entries of K/2 correspond to the positive weights w/2. Consequently\nQdetail is PSD and positive definite on e-perp. From (7.1),\n\n    Qdetail e=0,\n    t^T Hclock t=sum_i gamma_i h_i^2=Xreq/2,\n    Hnull t=0, t^T Qdetail t=Xreq.                (7.3)\n\nRetain the same terminal pool and late common column, and REPLACE\n\n    zeta by zeta_new=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t,\n    P by Pnew=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d by dnew=zeta_new+F(v-z).\n\nUse (4.6) with Kt replaced by Qdetail t, d replaced by dnew, and\nrvec=Qdetail t+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target. This again is a literal skew\ncompletion and, with (3.3), specifies EVERY original multiplier.\nEquations(7.3) give e^T zeta_new=0 and t^T zeta_new=theta, so both\nincoming-load compatibility moments remain ellload. The incoming\noptimum vector is exactly nu_target; it is not a free unpriced correction.\n\nThe complete polynomial is (3.4) with K,zeta replaced by\nQdetail,zeta_new. In particular its paid part equals\n\n    (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)^T Qdetail\n                   (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)\n        +(q-p^2-theta^2/(4Xreq))||x_0||^2.         (7.4)\n\nThis equality follows by expanding the square and using (7.3).\nNecessity of this same charge follows with scalar x_0=1,\ny=-(theta/(2Xreq))*t+c0 e, choosing c0 to cancel the common square,\nand choosing the independent g_n to cancel the terminal square.\nThus the exact global charge remains\n\n    q>=p^2+theta^2/(4Xreq),                       (7.5)\n\nwhich passes at qtar by (4.4). There is no extra hidden initial cost,\nno omitted clock energy, and no uncancelled terminal square.\n\nAll optimum signs pass for this second law as well. Hnull t=0, and\nLtri^T Kt has strictly positive entries by the argument following (4.6).\nFor Hclock t write aedge_i=gamma_i h_i>0, with aedge_(-1)=aedge_l=0.\nThe i-th coordinate of Hclock t is aedge_(i-1)-aedge_i; its suffix\nafter i is aedge_i. (7.6)\n\nThus this mechanism changes the first diagonal and paid source column,\nnot just a row-clock summary. Clock-free details also alter diagonals:\nthe tau_0 term in (7.6) must be included, even though Hnull t=0.\nThe full failure of this enlarged law follows from the next global\ninequality, which covers considerably more freedom than (7.2).\n\n### 7.1 A source-capacity inequality for the entire PSD-detail class\n\nFor this necessary-condition argument allow ANY symmetric PSD Qdetail\nwith Qdetail e=0; no such matrix is asserted to solve a feasibility\nproblem. Hence\n\n    nu_0+2alpha_0 <= N_00 <= b^2 q/a.             (7.10)\n\nThis bounds the source load by the ENTIRE paid initial budget.\nIt cannot be repaired by moving clock energy between details,\nusing clock-annihilating details, changing their ranks or supports,\nchanging zeta, changing z (including its clock), or changing skew\ncorrections while keeping the large source load and small q.\nIt even allows a different terminal distribution with nonnegative\nalpha_0, as long as the displayed architecture and p,s apply.\n\nFor the physical parameters, b^2/a<(17/12)^2/(2/5)<6 and qtar<p.\nThus, whenever q<=qtar,\n\n    N_00<6p<1/128,\n    N_00-(1/64)-2alpha_0<-1/128                  (7.11)\n\nif alpha_0>=0. This proves a uniform failed ORIGINAL first column\nfor the explicit enlarged law (7.2), and for the full class in this\nsubsection retaining nu_0=1/64. It is independent of all compatible\nskew corrections. Equivalently that fixed load requires q>1/384,\nwhereas qtar<p<2^(-40).\n\nFor clarity, the obstruction includes arbitrary finite nonnegative\nweights on the literal vectors gdetail_i,ddetail_i, arbitrary positive\nvariance metrics, their sums, and any other zero-sum PSD detail\nconstruction. Only necessary inequalities are claimed for this class;\nthere is no arbitrary unknown PSD object adopted as a certificate.\n\n### 7.2 Moment accounting for the surviving enlargement freedoms\n\nA general sum H=sum gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T\n+sum tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T, with nonnegative scalar weights,\nhas He=0, t^T Ht=sum gamma_i h_i^2, and its second sum annihilates t.\nIf its clock energy Eclock is less than Xreq, the explicit replacement\nQdetail=(1-Eclock/Xreq)K+H has total clock energy Xreq.\nFor zeta=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t the exact charge is again (7.5),\nand the incoming total and clock are again ellload. This describes\nthe larger parameter class covered by (7.10); (7.2) is its single\nfully fixed original-index instance used in this report.\n\nIf all details annihilate both e and t and one leaves K,zeta fixed,\nthen the optimum vectors and load moments remain unchanged, but\nN_00 decreases by a H_00, N_ll increases by H_ll, and the last-row\nclock defect changes by -hgap H_ll. The latter follows from\nH t=0, mu unchanged, and the last row of Ltri^T being e_l^T.\nThese diagonal and endpoint changes still have to be paid; null\nclock energy is not permission to ignore them.\n\nIf one changes z, alpha, or the incoming vector beyond the fixed\nlaw, the general compatibility equations are\n\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t,\n    V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, X=t^T Qdetail t,\n    theta=t^T zeta.\n\nThus changing nu_0 is a real global-moment change. This is not an improving terminal witness and does not refute the desired full segment or full-W bound.", "evidence": "# Exposed affine-height segments: reduction, quantitative regimes, and reactivation\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926,\ninvocation86 after ended85, controller cap1. Steering: TASK.md and\nmaster_guidance bb8c7f51e22c165f; plan b3ae4588b90dfc98.\n\nNo new fact is asserted. All new mathematical assertions below are parts of\nthis single joint derivation. No fact_submit, mathematical program, numerical\nevaluation, trajectory replay, optimizer, consult, or subagent was used.\nAdministrative text operations and literature/memory retrieval were used.\n\nThe **complete exposed-segment quantitative profile remains OPEN**. This\nreport does not call a scalar recurrence or a list of cases a solution. It\ncloses several uniform regimes and gives a legal all-m obstruction to an\nattempted simplification. Mechanism1 has not achieved its decisive closure,\nso the gated triangle mechanism was not opened. The original target remains\nOPEN and no complete minimax resolution is claimed.\n\n## 1. Fixed parameters, accepted inputs, and scope\n\nFix any integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME parameters\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1.\n\nHere H_j and a,c are local schedule notation, as in the assignment; D is\nthe accepted fact's D_m. Every physical update of H_m, including its last\nleaf 1+a, is retained. There is no child reset of m or eps.\n\nAccepted fact d8b59c7d92425121 says that the original full finite-word\nsupremum W equals the supremum over finite-dimensional affine-height\npolytope envelopes with the stated vertex bound, strict exposing height,\nand nonzero initial point perpendicular to the height vector. Their\ngradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), and their unique minimizer is0. We use that\nfact only for this legality and representation scope. It does not reduce\nthe full supremum to segments and gives no quantitative rate.\n\nAccepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the benchmark comparison and its\nconditional 799/800 reduction only after a full-class bound at17/(16D).\nNo converse to that implication is used here.\n\nThe length and mass identities needed below follow directly from the\nliteral concatenation. In particular\n\n    eta=a/(c-2), 0<eta<1,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2, and D<2*c^m.                       (1)\n\nIndeed c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c(c-2)>1>beta, so0<eta<1.\nThe displayed A_j starts at1 and satisfies A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.\nSince c^j>2^j for j>0, A_m<c^m. Also A_j>eta*c^j, proving\nthe bound on d_j/A_j, including j=0. For the numerical rational ranges\nused only in symbolic comparisons below,\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 0<a<1/2.               (2)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<17/12 and eps<1/1000 give\nc<(29/12)(1001/1000)<5/2. These are uniform exact inequalities,\nnot computed horizon checks.\n\n## 2. Scalar envelope, boundaries, gradient, and minimum\n\nLet L>=0, K=[0,L]e_1 in R^2, lambda=(1,T) with T>0. Minimizing in\nY=(y,0) gives\n\n    Phi(x,z)=phi_L(x)+z^2/2,\n    phi_L(x)= min_(0<=y<=L) [y+(x-y)^2/2],\n    y(x)=clip(x-1,0,L),\n    g_L(x)=phi_L'(x)=x-y(x).                           (3)\n\nHere L denotes normalized segment length, not the advertised smoothness\nconstant. All calculations use advertised smoothness1. Multiplying Phi\nby any original smoothness constant L_orig>0 and using steps h_i/L_orig\nleaves the trajectory and the normalized W=2R score unchanged.\n\nThe minimizer y(x) follows either from the derivative 1+y-x or from the\nprojection normal. Substitution gives the complete formula\n\n    phi_L(x)=x^2/2,                  x<=1,\n             x-1/2,                1<=x<=1+L,\n             L+(x-L)^2/2,          x>=1+L.             (4)\n\nAt x=1 the adjacent values are1/2 and the adjacent derivatives are1.\nAt x=1+L the adjacent values areL+1/2 and the derivatives again are1.\nFor L=0 the two exterior formulas coincide with x^2/2 and the middle\ninterval is a point. Thus g_L is continuous and nondecreasing, with\nsuccessive slopes1,0,1. Integrating its slope on a partition at the two\nbreakpoints proves global1-Lipschitz continuity. Consequently phi_L is\nconvex, differentiable, and1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, its\nminimum is0, and\n\n    0<=phi_L(x)<=x^2/2                                (5)\n\nbecause y=0 is always an available trial in (3).\n\nThe projection statement retains ALL normals: for every x, the selected\ny(x) satisfies (x-1-y(x))(v-y(x))<=0 for every v in[0,L]. The vertical\ncomponent of X-lambda does not affect projection onto this horizontal\nsegment. Hence grad Phi=(g_L(x),z)=X-P_K(X-lambda) at every point,\nincluding every original query, the terminal, and0. One fixed global\nfunction is used throughout, so no separate row-feasibility oracle is\nneeded. The unique minimum of Phi is also0 at(0,0).\n\n## 3. All exposed segments in all dimensions and exact orthogonality\n\nEvery nondegenerate segment allowed by the accepted representation has\nthe form K=[0,ell]e with ell>0 and e a unit vector. In fact0 cannot be an\ninterior point of a nondegenerate segment on which a linear functional is\nstrictly positive away from0: the two opposite directions would have\nopposite heights. Thus0 is an endpoint. Write\n\n    lambda=alpha*e+w, w perpendicular to e, alpha>0,\n    X=alpha*(x*e+z), z perpendicular to e,\n    L=ell/alpha, u=w/alpha.\n\nDirect substitution into the defining minimum, with y=alpha*t, yields\n\n    Phi(X)=alpha^2*(phi_L(x)+||z||^2/2).               (6)\n\nThe normalized coordinates obey exactly the original advertised\nsmoothness1 updates\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_L(x_i),\n    z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)*z_i.                             (7)\n\nThe factor alpha^2 cancels in the normalized objective/radius quotient.\nInitial orthogonality is precisely\n\n    x_0+<u,z_0>=0.                                   (8)\n\nThis accounts for all dimensions and all orientations, as follows.\n\n* If u=0, (8) forces x_0=0. The horizontal coordinate stays0 and any\n  admissible nonzero initialization is purely perpendicular quadratic.\n  In dimension1 there is no such nonzero initialization.\n* If u!=0, put T=||u||. All solutions of (8) with x_0=s are\n  z_0=-(s/T^2)u+v, where v is perpendicular to u. Therefore\n  ||z_0||^2=s^2/T^2+||v||^2. The canonical two-dimensional realization\n  is v=0: lambda=(1,T), X_0=(s,-s/T).\n* If s=0, horizontal motion is identically0. Every admissible nonzero\n  z_0, including one perpendicular to u in higher dimension, has the\n  pure quadratic score.\n* For K={0}, the function is ||X||^2/2 regardless of lambda. Every\n  admissible nonzero orthogonal initialization has that same score.\n  If a particular ambient dimension and lambda admit no such point,\n  they simply contribute no instance.\n\nIn particular a one-dimensional nondegenerate interval with scalar\northogonality is not an admissible nonzero start. The extra direction\nand its norm payment cannot be omitted.\n\n## 4. The full-word quadratic factor and the exact weighted score\n\nDefine\n\n    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j), p_0=1,\n    Q=p_m^2.\n\nFor j>=1 the SIGNED product of all quadratic factors1-h in H_j is\n-p_j. Indeed the length is odd, the leaf factor is-a, and\np_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2, which is satisfied by the displayed formula.\nEvery nonempty prefix of H_j has product modulus at most a<1.\nTo prove this, the leaf case is a; a prefix lying in the first child\nuses the induction hypothesis, and one passing its bridge has modulus\nat most\n\n    p_j*d_j = c^(-1)*beta^(2^j) <=a\n\ntimes a prefix modulus at most1 of the last child. Empty prefixes have\nmodulus1. In particular this also proves the weaker all-prefix bound1.\n\nLet x_n(L,s) be the actual scalar output of all H_m. For s!=0 put\n\n    q(L,s)=2*phi_L(x_n(L,s))/s^2.\n\nEquations (6)-(7), the true minimum0, and the full product give exactly\n\n    score = [s^2*q(L,s)+||z_0||^2*Q]/[s^2+||z_0||^2]. (9)\n\nThis is a weighted average with the TRUE squared initial radius. The\ncanonical two-dimensional score is\n\n    q_T(L,s)=[T^2*q(L,s)+Q]/[T^2+1].                 (10)\n\nExtra dimensions only add quadratic weight. Equations (8)-(10), and\nthe s=0 cases above, show that the unrestricted exposed-segment supremum\nis exactly the supremum of q(L,s) over L>=0 and real s!=0. One direction\nuses the weighted average and the fact that L=0 already supplies Q;\nthe other uses finite T tending to infinity for each fixed scalar\ninstance. This equality is only for the segment subclass.\n\nThere is also an explicit FINITE tilt rule for a potential strict\nwitness. If q(L,s)=q_*>b, then\n\n    T^2=1+2*(b-Q)_+/(q_*-b)                          (11)\n\ngives q_T>b. This follows on multiplying by T^2+1 from\nT^2(q_*-b)>b-Q. Also\n|q_T-q_*|=|Q-q_*|/(T^2+1). A limiting scalar score alone is not\npresented as a strict admissible witness.\n\n## 5. Complete small-start and degenerate-plateau regimes\n\nFor every L>=0 and |s|<=1, all pure quadratic candidate iterates have\nabsolute value at most |s| by the prefix product bound. They therefore\nremain in x<=1, where (3) has g_L(x)=x. This verifies the candidate\ntrajectory by induction through EVERY physical step. Its terminal is\n\n    x_n=-p_m*s, q(L,s)=Q for 0<|s|<=1.               (12)\n\nFor L=0 this identity holds for every real s!=0. The zero scalar start\nis handled by Section3 and does not require dividing by s.\n\nEquation (1) also gives\n\n    D*Q <2*c^(-m)<1/4, and p_m<1/(2*sqrt(D)),         (13)\n\nbecause beta<1, c>2 and m>=1536>=3. Thus these complete regimes are\nstrictly below1/D, and (9) preserves this assertion in all dimensions.\n\n## 6. Every no-crossing or one-crossing trajectory, all L and amplitudes\n\nHere a strict crossing means consecutive nonzero iterates with opposite\nsigns; a zero hit remains0 thereafter. The argument is supplied jointly\nand actually applies to any one-dimensional convex1-smooth function\nwith unique minimizing point0 and minimum value0. We apply it only to\nthe present family.\n\nOn either fixed ray let t be the radius and G(t) the positive gradient\nmagnitude. For a ray sign sigma in{-1,1}, write f_sigma(t)=f(sigma*t)\nand G(t)=sigma*f'(sigma*t). Then G is nondecreasing,1-Lipschitz,\nG(0)=0 and G(t)<=t.\nIf q=G(t), one has G(v)<=min(v,q) for0<=v<=t, whence\n\n    2*f_sigma(t)<=2*t*q-q^2.                        (14)\n\nFor a segment of actual updates staying on that ray, with total physical\nmass T, initial radius R and final radius t, radii decrease. All queried\ngradient magnitudes are at least q=G(t), so R>=t+T*q. The identity\n\n    (t+T*q)^2-(1+2T)*(2*t*q-q^2)\n                         =(t-(T+1)*q)^2>=0\n\nproves\n\n    2*f(terminal)<=R^2/(1+2T).                     (15)\n\nThis includes intervals wholly in the outer quadratic region, intervals\nthat traverse either boundary, and arbitrary L. It is not a substitution\nof a clipped-gradient formula for g_L.\n\nSuppose there is exactly one strict crossing, at physical step h=1+d_k.\nLet P,T be the COMPLETE masses before and after that step. If its query\nhas radius y and gradient magnitude v, the no-crossing prefix gives\n|s|>=y+P*v>=(1+P)*v. Its crossing landing has radius\nz=h*v-y<=d_k*v. Applying (15) to its actual no-crossing suffix yields\n\n    q(L,s)<= d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                (16)\n\nThe chronological hierarchy pays this denominator at every possible\nphysical occurrence. Put A=A_k, d=d_k,\nN=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0 and x=P-S_k. The full adjacent H_k children imply\n\n    0<=x<=N, T=S_k+N-x,\n    (1+P)^2*(1+2T)=(A+x)^2*(2A-1+2N-2x),\n    D=4A+2d-1+2N.\n\nThe derivative of the denominator expression is\n2(A+x)(A-1+2N-3x). It has no interior minimum. Its value at N minus\nits value at0 is\n\n    N*[2A(A-1)+N*(2A-1)]>=0.\n\nHence the denominator is at least A^2*(2A-1+2N). Since d/A<1/2,\n\n    d^2*(4A+2d-1) <(A^2/4)*(5A-1)\n                            <=A^2*(2A-1),\n\nwhere the last inequality is equivalent to A>=1. Adding the2N terms\npreserves strictness since d^2<A^2. Thus (16) is strictly less than1/D.\nA trajectory with no strict crossing uses (15) on the whole word and\nhas score at most1/D. A zero hit gives zero scalar terminal value.\n\nThis closes ALL finite-L trajectories with at most one strict crossing,\nwithout prescribing where either curvature boundary is visited. It does\nnot cover arbitrary repeated crossings: even the small-start quadratic\ntrajectories in (12) cross at every positive step, so crossing counts\nmust not be silently assumed small.\n\n## 7. Complete forcing identity and uniform thin-segment bounds\n\nFor each physical query let y_i=clip(x_i-1,0,L), so0<=y_i<=L. The\nactual recurrence can be unrolled algebraically, with no branch selected\nor evaluated:\n\n    x_n=-p_m*s+sum_(i<n) w_i*y_i,\n    w_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t).                  (17)\n\nLet E_j be the sum of absolute values of these forcing coefficients for\nthe complete word H_j. Literal concatenation gives E_0=0 and\n\n    E_(j+1)=(1+d_j*p_j)*E_j+(1+d_j)*p_j.\n\nThis recurrence is SOLVED here, not left as a quantitative answer. Its shared glossary\nentry is C_seg_j.\n\nUsing p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 shows\n\n    E_(j+1)+p_(j+1)+1\n               =(1+d_j*p_j)*(E_j+p_j+1),\n    E_j=2*C_j-1-p_j.                                (19)\n\nThe signed sum telescopes to1- product(1-h)=1+p_j for j>=1.\nConsequently the total POSITIVE coefficient mass in (17) is C_m,\nand the total magnitude of its NEGATIVE coefficients is C_m-1-p_m.\nKeeping these signs gives, for every actual scalar trajectory,\n\n    -p_m*s-L*(C_m-1-p_m) <=x_n<= -p_m*s+L*C_m,\n    |x_n|<=p_m*|s|+L*C_m.                            (20)\n\nThe y_i were not independently optimized to claim realizability: (20)\nis only an upper bound on the actual coupled sequence.\n\nThe product (18) has the following completely evaluated uniform bound:\n\n    C_m<6*sqrt(m).                                   (21)\n\nTo prove it take K=ceil(log_2(4m)). Since2^m>=4m for m>=4, K<=m.\nAlso2^K<8m. For k<K, each factor in (18) is at most1+1/c<sqrt(2),\nso their product is less than sqrt(8m). For k=K+t,\n\n    c^(-1)*beta^(2^k) <(1/2)*exp(-2^t),\n\nusing log(1-eps)<=-eps and2^K>=4m. The tail product is at most\nexp((1/2)*sum_(t>=0)exp(-2^t)). The full product is less than2*sqrt(8m)<6*sqrt(m).\nIf the tail is empty the same bound holds.\n\nCombining (5), (12), and (20) proves the all-start bound\n\n    sup_(s!=0) q(L,s) <= [p_m+L*C_m]^2. In particular it closes the entire thin-segment\nregime\n\n    0<=L<=1/(12*sqrt(m*D)):\n                 sup_(s!=0) q(L,s)<1/D. The following definitions and proofs are reproduced together; no unverified result is imported as a separate premise. The conclusion refutes an amplitude lemma, not an objective profile.\n\n## 1. Same word, fixed function, and precise conclusions\n\nFix an integer m>=1536. At EVERY child use the SAME parameters\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    S=S_m, D=D_m=1+2S, n=2^m-1,\n    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.\n\nWrite h_i,0<=i<n, for the chronological entries of H_m. Its last physical\nentry is ell; d_m is auxiliary and is not an additional physical step.\n\nFix0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa. Keep one static function throughout:\n\n    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,\n    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,\n    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r) for r>=0.\n\nThe actual updates are x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), x_0=s!=0.\nPsi_j(s) is the actual complete H_j output on this fixed function, and\nM_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|. The objective payoff is\n2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2. Its denominator is the TRUE initial distance squared;\nan interval input bound R is never substituted for s in an objective claim.\n\n**Mechanism1: complete stated domain.** If\n\n    kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1,                          (1.1)\n\nthen EVERY nonzero start s and EVERY B>=kappa satisfy\n\n    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n       <=[1+21/(4m)]/D <17/(16D).                  (1.2)\n\nThe error constant21/4 is absolute, independent of kappa,B,start,m.\nThe statement includes equality in(1.1), arbitrarily small kappa,\nall repeated exterior crossings, and final same-side middle-to-core entry.\n\n**Mechanism2: exact counterexample to the proposed stronger lemma.**\n\n    G(M_j(d_j*w))<=a*w for every0<w<=B              (1.3)\n\nis FALSE. For EVERY enclosing m>=1536 choose the fixed allowed law\nkappa=B=1, j=m, and w_m=A_(m-1)/d_m. Then\n\n    0<w_m<beta<1,\n    G(M_m(d_m*w_m))=theta=a*beta>a*w_m.             (1.4)\n\nThese are exact complete-word interval images attained by compatible\nactual starts. This is not a local-step comparison failure.\n\nFor a possible repair, the necessary coefficient loss on this witness is\n\n    L_m=beta*d_m/A_(m-1),\n    1<L_m<1+3/(2m),\n    lim_(m->infinity) m*(L_m-1)=(3+2sqrt(2))/4.     (1.5)\n\nThus a proposed universal coefficient a*(1+C/m) requires\nC>=(3+2sqrt(2))/4. This is a NECESSARY loss, not a sufficient upper\nbound for all amplitudes or for the general kink law.\n\nThe counterexample has first inner reversing level J=0. It refutes\nthe assertion quantified over arbitrary J, but does not settle positive J.\nNo third mechanism or asymmetric family is entered.\n\n\n## 2. Actual function, objective areas, and all interpolation rows\n\nThe radial objective is exactly\n\n    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                         0<=r<=1,\n    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                   1<=r<=u,\n    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                       r>=u,\n    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa).                       (2.1)\n\nValues and first derivatives agree at both knots. At B=kappa the\nmiddle interval disappears and u=1. At kappa=1 this is the Huber law\nwith cap B; the family kink radius has not been changed.\n\nThe odd derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with piecewise\nslopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning an interval at the knots proves\n|g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Hence f is finite, convex, differentiable, and\nglobally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0 and\nits minimum is0. The true initial distance is |s|.\n\nAll ordered smooth-convex rows of actual samples hold directly. For x<y\nput Delta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. On[x,y],\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).\n\nIntegrating gives both directed convexity remainders at least Delta^2/2.\nConsequently, with g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), for EVERY ordered pair of\nphysical, terminal, or optimum points,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.   (2.2)\n\nRepeated points have identical values/derivatives. The Gram matrix is\npositive semidefinite because each quadratic form is a square of a real\nlinear combination of the actual scalar vectors. No terminal gradient\nor physical leaf is deleted.\n\nAt radius r, put p=G(r). Since p<=r and G(t)<=min(t,p) on[0,r],\n\n    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                               (2.3)\n\nAlso G(r)<=min(r,B), so F(r)<=J_B(r), where J_B denotes the Huber\nfunction with derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B). This value comparison does\nNOT imply a universal comparison of individual kink-function steps.\n\nFor advertised smoothness L>0, objective L*f and steps h_i/L give\nthe same points and normalized payoff. A smaller actual core curvature\nnever changes the physical schedule.\n\n\n## 3. Exact same-m masses and uniform elementary bounds\n\nThroughout,\n\n    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>99/100,\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2, 2/5<a<1/2,\n    0<theta<1/2, c*theta=beta^2<1.                 (3.1)\n\nUse7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000 for\n12/5<c<(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7.\nThen a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5, while a<1/2 since c>2.\n\nDefine\n\n    t=c*(c-2), eta=a/(c-2)=beta/t.\n\nSince lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, t>1>beta and0<eta<1.\nThe literal word recursion and its exact solution are\n\n    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j, j>=0.                 (3.2)\n\nSubstitution verifies the formula and A_0=1. Hence\n\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    A_j>d_j/(c-2), d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2 for j>=0.      (3.3)\n\nThe second strict inequality follows from the positive term\n(1-eta)*2^j.\n\nLet d=d_(m-1). By(3.1), d>(2/5)*2^(m-1)=2^m/5.\nFor all integers m>=8, 2^m>20m: it holds at8, and doubling preserves\nthe inequality because2m>=m+1. Thus\n\n    d>4m=1/epsilon, 1/d<epsilon,\n    D=2A_m-1<2c^m=2d_m/a.                         (3.4)\n\nThe exact expansion\n\n    t=1+2lambda*(lambda-1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2\n\nand6<2lambda*(lambda-1)=4+2sqrt(2)<7, lambda^2<6 give\n\n    1+6epsilon<t<1+8epsilon.                       (3.5)\n\nHere epsilon<1/6 bounds6epsilon^2 by epsilon.\n\nAt every physical level-k bridge, the actual original prefix mass P\nsatisfies P>=S_k, because its full left H_k child is present.\nThe actual suffix after that bridge is H_k followed by zero or more\npairs(h_l^lev,H_l) with strictly increasing ancestor levels l>k.\nAscend the binary tree, retaining an ancestor exactly when its bridge\nand right child remain ahead. This accounts for every remaining physical\nleaf, including the terminal leaf, without resetting m.\n\n\n## 5. Complete unit-Huber reference, proved jointly\n\nFirst set B=1 and use J_1. Its exact one-step interval radius for\nh=1+d_h>=1 is\n\n    V_h(R)=max(d_h*min(R,1), R-h).                 (5.1)\n\nThe odd continuous step map has extrema at an endpoint or a knot.\nIt maps[-R,R] ONTO the symmetric interval with this radius.\nLet U_j(R) be the exact interval radius for complete H_j. Then\n\n    U_(j+1)=U_j composed with V_(h_j^lev) composed with U_j. (5.2)\n\nAll radius maps are nondecreasing. Composition is exact, since a continuous\nodd map has a connected symmetric interval image. Thus no incompatible\nchoice of separate child starts occurs.\n\nFirst prove by induction\n\n    U_j(A_j)=1,\n    U_j(R)=R-S_j for R>=A_j,\n    U_j(R)<=max(1,R-S_j) for R>=0.                  (5.3)\n\nThe empty word has these properties. At input A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,\nthe first child gives A_j+d_j+1. The bridge has staying output A_j\nand crossing output at most d_j<A_j. The second child gives1.\nA positive exterior start R>=A_(j+1) stays saturated at every query\nand through the terminal, giving R-S_(j+1). Monotonicity bounds smaller\ninterval inputs by1. This proves(5.3).\n\nNext prove simultaneously, for every integer r>=0,\n\n    U_j(d_j*theta^r)<=theta^(r+1), j>=1.            (5.4)\n\nFor j=1, d_1=beta<1 and the output is exactly\na*beta*theta^r=theta^(r+1). At the unscaled parent input c*d_j,\n(5.3) bounds the first child by max(1,c*d_j-S_j). The bridge's\ncrossing term is at most d_j. Its staying term is at most d_j because\n\n    c*d_j-S_j-(1+d_j)<=d_j\n    iff (c-2)*d_j<=A_j,\n\nwhich follows from(3.3). Apply the second child's r=0 bound.\nFor r>=1, c*theta<1 gives\nc*d_j*theta^r<=d_j*theta^(r-1). The first child gives at most theta^r<1,\nthe bridge at most d_j*theta^r, and the second child at most\ntheta^(r+1). This proves the full family(5.4).\n\nFor0<R<=d_m, choose r>=0 with\nd_m*theta^(r+1)<R<=d_m*theta^r. Monotonicity implies\n\n    U_m(R)<=theta^(r+1)<R/d_m.                    (5.5)\n\nFor an actual strict crossing at level k, the landing radius is at most\nd_k. If k>=1, the following full H_k gives radius<=theta<a by(5.4);\nif k=0, the landing itself is<=a. At each later retained ancestor l,\nan incoming radius<=a<1 becomes at most a*d_l<=d_l at the bridge,\nand the full right H_l gives<=theta. The exact suffix from Section3\ntherefore proves\n\n    any strict crossing in H_m => terminal radius<=a. (5.6)\n\nAll repeated crossings and all remaining leaves are included.\n\nFor R<=d_m use2J_1(r)<=r^2 and(5.5) to bound payoff by1/d_m^2.\nFor R>=d_m with a crossing use(5.6) to bound it by a^2/d_m^2.\nBoth are less than1/D, since D<2d_m/a and d_m>2/a by(3.1),(3.4).\nNo-crossing histories obey(4.1), giving1/D.\nThus the complete all-start Huber profile is<=1/D.\n\nThe start R=D=1+2S stays saturated through terminal radius1+S\nand has payoff[2(1+S)-1]/D^2=1/D. The exact profile is therefore1/D.\nScaling this reference Huber function by B gives the same profile for\nevery cap B>0 on the same physical word. This reference calculation does\nnot change the cap or kink of an actual outward-kink trajectory.\n\n\n## 8. Mechanism2: an exact complete-word counterexample\n\nSet kappa=B=1. Then G(r)=min(r,1), the actual function is J_1,\nand M_j=U_j. This is one fixed member of the required family.\nThe first inner reversing level J is0 because ell>1.\n\nWe first prove the exact identity\n\n    U_j(d_j)=theta for EVERY j>=1.                 (8.1)\n\nIts upper bound is(5.4), proved in this joint report.\nAt j=1 equality holds since d_1=beta<1.\nPut Delta_j=d_j-A_(j-1). At j=2,\n\n    Delta_2=beta*c-2-a\n           =epsilon*(2/c-lambda*epsilon)>0.       (8.2)\n\nSubstitute c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/(lambda*(1+epsilon))\nand lambda-1/lambda=2 to check the equality. For the sign,\nc<5/2 gives2/c>4/5, while lambda*epsilon<1/400.\nFor every j>=2 the exact recursion gives\n\n    Delta_(j+1)=2Delta_j+(c-2-1/c)*d_j>0,          (8.3)\n\nbecause t=c*(c-2)>1. Thus d_j>A_(j-1) for j>=2.\n\nAssume(8.1) at j-1. At interval input A_(j-1), the first H_(j-1)\ngives radius1 by(5.3), the bridge gives d_(j-1) by(5.1), and the\nsecond child gives theta by induction. Hence U_j(A_(j-1))=theta.\nMonotonicity and d_j>A_(j-1) give the lower bound in(8.1).\nIn particular,\n\n    U_m(A_(m-1))=theta.                            (8.4)\n\nSet q=2/c<5/6. Since d_m=beta*c^(m-1), the proposed amplitude is\n\n    w_m=A_(m-1)/d_m\n       =[eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)]/beta.                (8.5)\n\nFrom(3.5) and epsilon<1/1000,\n\n    eta=beta/t>1/2,\n    0<1-eta=(t-beta)/t<9epsilon,\n    beta*t-1>(1-epsilon)*(1+6epsilon)-1\n                  =5epsilon-6epsilon^2>4epsilon.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    beta^2-eta=eta*(beta*t-1)>2epsilon.             (8.6)\n\nFurthermore(5/6)^4<1/2 and m-1>=12 give\n\n    (1-eta)*q^(m-1)<9epsilon/8<2epsilon.            (8.7)\n\nThus eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)<beta^2 and0<w_m<beta<1=B.\nThe actual interval input is d_m*w_m=A_(m-1), and theta<1.\nEquation(8.4) yields\n\n    G(M_m(d_m*w_m))=G(theta)=theta=a*beta>a*w_m,\n\nproving(1.4) for every enclosing m in the assignment.\n\nExact interval composition in Section5 ensures compatible actual starts,\nnot adversarially reset child inputs. An actual nonzero start in\n[-A_(m-1),A_(m-1)] attains terminal magnitude theta. For a precise\nselection take the least s in[0,A_(m-1)] with |Psi_m(s)|=theta.\nThe set is nonempty by(8.4) and oddness, compact by continuity, and\nits minimum is positive since Psi_m(0)=0. This specifies a single\ncomplete trajectory on the fixed function. No objective claim replaces\nthat true s by the interval radius.\n\n\n## 9. Exact necessary coefficient price; no sufficient repair claimed\n\nThe witness's ratio to the asserted right-hand side is\n\n    L_m=theta/(a*w_m)=beta/w_m\n       =beta^2/[eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)].              (9.1)\n\nSection8 gives L_m>1. Since the second denominator term is positive,\n\n    L_m<beta^2/eta=beta*t.\n\nThe exact expansion, using lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2), is\n\n    beta*t=1+lambda^2*epsilon-epsilon^2-lambda^2*epsilon^3\n           <1+6epsilon=1+3/(2m).                 (9.2)\n\nThis proves the finite necessary witness loss in(1.5).\nDirect subtraction gives\n\n    0<beta*t-L_m\n      =beta^2*(1-eta)*q^(m-1)\n         /[eta*(eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1))]\n      <36epsilon*(5/6)^(m-1),                    (9.3)\n\nusing eta>1/2 and1-eta<9epsilon. Multiplying this error by m sends it\nto0, while\n\n    m*(beta*t-1)=lambda^2/4-1/(16m)-lambda^2/(64m^2).\n\nTherefore lim m*(L_m-1)=lambda^2/4. Any universal amplitude estimate\nwith coefficient a*(1+C/m) must have L_m<=1+C/m on this witness,\nhence C>=lambda^2/4 is necessary.\n\nThis does not establish a sufficient paid amplitude theorem or control\naccumulated losses through arbitrary inner reversals. It is impermissible\nto compound an unproved per-level estimate using this calculation.\n\nThe complete objective profile of this same function is exactly1/D by\nSection5.
